> Counts such as `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` `(466)` record the **total at the moment that entry was written**, not the current total (later entries push it up — it is 502 now); they are kept verbatim so each entry still matches its own commit.

### v1.3.1 (unreleased)

> Increments after v1.3.0-r2; the `NOTICE_VERSION` sentinel moves to `1.3.1` in step (the notice content changed ⇒ users who saw the r2 notice will see it once more), and `package.json`'s `version` is bumped to `1.3.1` in step.

- **Overhaul: presets upgraded from a "glass-subset snapshot" to a "whole-configuration snapshot", plus preset import / export (ADR-0011)**.
  **Why**: presets used to snapshot only the glass family of keys, so after importing one a large share of settings did not follow — "a preset that does not feel like a preset". The user's ask was "let a saved preset carry this plugin's entire configuration", and that presets could move between users.
  **How**: the preset body now has four sections — the **settings section** (every look-and-behavior key, **always carried**, missing keys padded with defaults ⇒ the result of applying is deterministic) plus **three asset sections** (fonts / mascot artwork / session avatars, **carried on demand**). The key set is **derived from `KINDS`** (`lib/settings-schema.js`'s `PROFILE_PRESET_KEYS` / `PRESET_ASSET_KEYS` / `PRESET_EXCLUDED_KEYS`, mutually exclusive and exhaustive; content and runtime-memory keys go to the hard-exclusion table) — no second hand-copied whitelist, so a newly added settings key automatically joins the settings section. The body's schema tag becomes **`dsh-we/profile-preset@1`**. All seven factory presets are rebuilt on the new tag and scope ("Hacker Green (Fish)" is a read-only transcription of the author's own machine configuration). **Applying** becomes whole-batch: one merge of the settings section + assets placed by the host's `install-assets` (file names / display box written back; font values go through the font-set channel — font keys do not live in settings) + a **reapply-all** orchestrator `reapplyAll()` — the rotation switch / scene live rendering / fps-cap / playback-rate family is not on the `emit()` subscription chain, and skipping it means "written into the selection, nothing moves on screen". **The two "absent" semantics are opposites** (do not mix them up): an absent settings key pads to default; an absent asset section means that family of keys **stays untouched** (not carried ≠ cleared). **Both saving and exporting open the asset-check dialog first** (fonts checked / mascot checked / avatars unchecked by default — images roughly double the size), each row stating what happens when unchecked (no fonts → the receiver keeps whatever they currently have; no mascot → falls back to the built-in maid/whale; no avatars → falls back to the built-in default avatars); the checks only decide whether images and fonts come along — numeric settings are always saved in full. Images **never enter or leave** the browser: saving sends only the check list and the host embeds from the local data directory; applying only calls `install-assets` for file names — no second persistence channel is added.
  **Breaking**: **old presets are voided outright, with no compatibility layer** (the user's call: an old body holds only the glass subset, and interpreting it under the new semantics would produce "half preset, half current values"). An old-tag preset reads as "unreadable" — the panel names **the version as obsolete** and offers "save a fresh copy" as the way out; **presets saved before the upgrade must be saved again**. The seven factory presets were rebuilt with this change and are unaffected. The tombstone marker value is untouched (deleted factory presets do not come back).
  **New**: **export** (an "Export" button per row → the same asset checks → download the `.json`, served with an attachment header; no blob / `showSaveFilePicker`) and **import** (three local pre-checks, each with a decidable message: unreadable / not JSON / wrong `$schema` naming the tag it wants; success only adds it to the list and **does not apply it**).
  **Routes**: the `/glass-presets` family gains the `create` `embed` list, `<id>/install-assets`, `<id>/export?assets=…` and `import` endpoints (indexed in [`ROUTE-INDEX.md`](../ROUTE-INDEX.md), a generated artifact).
  **Verification**: `node test/verify-route-index.mjs` / `verify-i18n` / `verify-client` / `verify-package-files` / `verify-package-publish` exit 0; `node scripts/build-client.mjs` + `node test/verify-client-sync.mjs` all green (artifact matches source).

- **Change: the factory preset "Factory default" is replaced in place by the new "Hacker Green (Fish)" preset (the values from PR [#171](https://github.com/elysia395/dsh-wallpaper-engine/pull/171), applied as a **replacement** rather than an addition)**.
  **How**: `lib/glass-presets/factory-default.json` is rewritten in place (values and name) instead of adding PR #171's eighth preset (`factory-fish`) — this keeps the factory set at **seven**, so a fresh install still has one of the eight slots free for the user. The values match PR #171: identical to "Author's preset" (`factory-author`) except `sidebarContentColor`, which goes from `#0d1524` (dark content backing) to `#ffffff`; the other seven differences against `factory-author` are keys added by newer schema versions whose values are exactly the defaults, so they are omitted here in the sparse style of the existing factory presets and padded back by `sanitizeGlassPresetValues` on read.
  **Knock-on**: `src/glass-panel.js`'s `FACTORY_PRESET_CN` maps `factory-default` to `"黑客绿(Fish)"` (a factory name must be a literal in the glossary — the i18n condition is a text scan, and the `name` inside the data file is invisible to it); `src/i18n-copy.js` swaps the key accordingly (the old "出厂默认 / Factory default" pair is removed so it cannot become an orphan). `test/verify-presets.mjs`'s matching condition follows.
  **Blast radius**: the factory preset count is unchanged (7), the cap is unchanged (8), and no new settings key is added. **Client-only ⇒ a page refresh takes effect.** Presets a user saved under the "Factory default" name are unaffected (name-collision checks run against the active list, so renaming a factory preset does not invalidate saved user presets).

- **Fix: web wallpapers periodically stole input focus (issue [#148](https://github.com/elysia395/dsh-wallpaper-engine/issues/148)) — element-level focus fence + host-side focus hand-back (C11 + C12 merged)**.
  **Why**: scripts inside a web / scene wallpaper call `focus()` over and over, moving the caret out of the chat composer. The fence only made a **frame-level** decision (the wallpaper frame as a whole never gets keyboard focus), which cannot stop two kinds of grab: element-level `el.focus()` inside the wallpaper document, and the **cross-origin WindowProxy** paths `top.focus()` / `parent.focus()` plus host-document internals such as `autofocus` / `dialog.showModal()` / `label` forwarding — a guard living inside the wallpaper document cannot reach any of those.
  **Fix**: ① `lib/we-focus-guard.js` keeps the frame-level decision unchanged and adds an **element-level fence**: `HTMLElement.prototype.focus` is allowed only inside the `gestureWindowMs` (**1000 ms**) window after the last real interaction, and outside it the call is swallowed and counted (`window.__weFocusGuard`'s `elCalls` / `elAllowed` / `elBlocked` / `elLastBlockedAt` / `elLastBlockedStack`, so a wallpaper author can gather evidence when reproducing). **The gesture window cannot be dropped**: the wallpaper layer is `pointer-events: none`, so a real click on the wallpaper arrives as a shim-synthesised pointer / mouse event with `isTrusted === false` — an edit box that ships with the wallpaper taking focus from that click is legitimate behaviour, and a blanket block would destroy it (the plan's §3.5 lists that as a "do not do"). ② New `src/focus-handback.js` runs in the **host document** and hands focus back: the `focusout` came from a remembered element, `document.activeElement` is `iframe.we-iframe`, and there was no real gesture in the last 1000 ms ⇒ hand back; with `STALE_MS` / `MIN_GAP_MS` as two extra safety nets and `window.__weFocusHandback.{handbacks,skipped}` observable. ③ `scripts/build-client.mjs` registers the new module and `src/client.js`'s 2e section mounts it through `ctx.effect`. The host half is not optional: a guard in the wallpaper document cannot touch a cross-origin WindowProxy, nor stop `autofocus` / `dialog.showModal()` / `label` forwarding.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` groups ⑦–⑨ (frame level still swallows unconditionally + element level allowed inside / blocked outside the window + host-side hand-back) and `test/verify-client.mjs`'s host-half behaviour conditions (the real `src/focus-handback.js` on a `with(__scope)` mount rig), each with negative / positive controls.
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` / `verify:docs` / `npm run build` / `smoke` exit 0; `lib/client.js` rebuilt in this same round. **Both host and client changed ⇒ restart `dsh web`** (real-device reproduction on an author page is still pending on the author's side).
  **Guard trap (for next time)**: the first `src/focus-handback.js` only checked `typeof document === "undefined"`, while `test/verify-transcode-state.mjs` feeds a capability-incomplete stub document ⇒ `TypeError: document.addEventListener is not a function`, which broke the whole `apply`; it now checks capabilities method by method. The first `verify-client` host half was a **false green** (the hand-back rate floor is 300 ms and the condition block finishes well under 300 ms, so the assertion was really being stopped by `MIN_GAP_MS`) ⇒ really sleep 320 ms first and add the "after sleeping long enough, hand-back must resume" negative control.

- **Performance: an on-disk index for wallpaper-library scans plus a signature fast path — zero I/O after a restart when the library has not changed (issue [#158](https://github.com/elysia395/dsh-wallpaper-engine/issues/158) (b))**.
  **Why**: after a cold start / restart every request re-`stat`ed the whole wallpaper library (≈ 1.57 MB of synthetic payload for 2600 entries on this machine, and scanning is the one real TTFB cost), while the library does not change across the vast majority of restarts; the scan result lived only in process memory and died with the process.
  **Fix (host)**: `inventory-index.json` under `cacheBaseDir()` (`INDEX_VERSION` + `sig` + `builtAt` + the **raw scan material** `we` + a per-entry probe memory `probes`); the signature is `sha1(version + installDir + sorted libraryDirs + mtimeMs:size of every scan root)`. The four disciplines are the whole difficulty: ① the index stores the **raw scan material, not the payload** — `media` / `preview` / `frameUrl` are base64url of absolute paths from `tokenFor()` and `mediaMap` is per-process, so replaying an old payload would 404 every URL after a restart; replay has to go back through `assembleInventory`; ② a signature hit means zero fs (including zero `stat`); ③ a signature **mismatch does not wait** (the library itself changed, and serving the old payload would hand the user the previous library's inventory), concurrent requests share the one in-flight scan `rescanOnce`, and stale-while-revalidate only serves "signature unchanged, content may have changed" (index older than `INVENTORY_REVALIDATE_MS`); ④ the per-entry invalidation key is the project directory's mtime (`enumerateWallpapersAsync` grows a `dirAbs`), plus index GC. A corrupt index / version mismatch / wrong element shape ⇒ treat it as "no index", fall back to the cold path and self-heal, never throw.
  **Guards**: the new `test/verify-inventory-index.mjs` (**23 conditions**) in six groups — cold start (four existence probes + index written + raw material only) / restart with the library untouched (**0 probes, 0 stats**) / one new library entry (only the new one probed, `exists=2`, `stat=3`) / a single project directory changed (negative control) / index tolerance (bad JSON, old version, `we:[null]`, `null` mixed into the entry list) / GC.
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` (including the new guard) / `verify:docs` → `npm run verify:all` exit 0. **Host half changed ⇒ restart `dsh web`**.
  **Third hole fixed along the way**: `rescan()` dereferenced `w.fileAbs` straight off the raw list and threw `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'fileAbs')` (`lib/inventory.js:428`) for a `null` entry — the new guard surfaced it on its first run; the fix is a factory-level `liveEntries(list)` shape filter that both `assembleInventory` and `rescan` go through. (Response compression C8 and the startup path C10 are **deliberately not done** this round: on this machine the 2600-entry payload measures identity 6.8 ms / gzip-6 11.1 ms (101,682 B) / brotli-4 9.1 ms (78,799 B) ⇒ compression is a net loss over loopback and happens after scanning, so it does not help TTFB; C10's artifact cost measures ≈ 12 ms (read 0.4 + gunzip 4.7 + parse 6.9) and splitting "critical CSS synchronously + the rest after first frame" carries a visible-flash risk that needs a real-device A/B.)

- **New capability: per-theme glass colour — one colour for both themes, with a separate dark one (issue [#159](https://github.com/elysia395/dsh-wallpaper-engine/issues/159) ②)**.
  **Why**: `glassColor` used to be a scalar hex, so light and dark themes had to share one glass tint — which often reads too bright or muddy in dark mode — and its shape disagreed with the existing `themeColors` + `themeDarkSeparate` pair, leaving users no way to say "set the dark one separately".
  **Fix**: `glassColor` is upgraded from a scalar to a `{ light, dark }` pair (`lib/settings-schema.js`'s `KINDS.glassColor.kind = 'glassColors'`, `DEFAULTS.glassColor = GLASS_COLOR_DEFAULTS`) and a new `glassDarkSeparate` boolean appears (off by default, both sides equal ⇒ the factory state is still "one colour for both"); `SETTINGS_VERSION` **5 → 6**, `migrateSettings` turns a scalar into two equal sides, and `readGlassColors(raw)` always returns a complete pair (a broken side falls back to the good one, a string gives two equal sides) — read tolerance and migration are two layers, because preset JSON carries a scalar and stamps the current version, skipping the migration path, so dropping read tolerance would turn factory presets into a white tint. `src/effects.js` gains a module-level `glassColorOf(selection, theme)`, the four `--we-surface-tint-{light,dark}` / `-rgb-{light,dark}` each take their own half, and `--we-glass-color` stays the **light** scalar; `src/glass.js`'s conversation bar takes each half by theme. The settings page and the sidebar's Appearance pane draw the second row 「玻璃颜色 · 深色」 only while the switch is on (`src/glass-panel.js`'s `panelGlassPair(sel)` / `panelThemeIsDark()` + `switchRow("深色单独设置")`). **The sidebar deliberately stays theme-blind**: `--we-sidebar-color` is an inline body style that cannot beat a stylesheet re-declaration, and its consumers have no `data-ds-dark-theme` twin ⇒ the sidebar surface has one swatch that writes the current theme's side (what you see is what you edit), and the guards pin that down explicitly as the exception to per-theme.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-presets.mjs`'s golden fixture grows to 18 cases (`glassColor` pair + `glassDarkSeparate` + `settingsVersion: 6`), `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` covers panel normalisation and the sidebar surface, `test/verify-client.mjs`'s behaviour rig covers both light / dark values and the both-sides-equal state with the switch off.
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` / `verify:docs` / `npm run build` / `smoke` → `npm run verify:all` exit 0; `lib/client.js` rebuilt in this same round. **Both the host settings table and the client changed ⇒ restart `dsh web`** (`config.json` migrates to v6 on first start).
  **Guard trap (for next time)**: a "the two values are unequal" per-theme condition is a **false green** — each side passes through its own brightness clamp, so the outputs are unequal anyway ⇒ use a **causal** condition (compare two baselines built with the same colour on both sides); `--we-surface-tint-light === PAIR.light` is a **false red** (the sandbox's real `weClampSurfaceColor` shadows the identity stub); the first migration guard was a false green (read tolerance covered the read exit); `panelThemeIsDark()` must check capabilities one by one for a stub document (has a body, no `hasAttribute`).

- **Fix: the scrollbar no longer looks detached from the plugin's tint (issue [#157](https://github.com/elysia395/dsh-wallpaper-engine/issues/157)) — the four host scrollbar tokens now carry the plugin's glass colour**.
  **Why**: the host scrollbar family goes through the four base tokens `--dsw-alias-scrollbar-{bg,hover}-l{1,2}`, and roughly 17 local re-declarations in the host all write the **indirect layer** `var(--dsw-alias-scrollbar-bg-l2)`; inside the plugin's glass pages those four still held the host's neutral colours ⇒ the scrollbar looked dirty against tinted glass and belonged to a different palette than the page.
  **Fix**: instead of patching styles per anchor, **swap the four base tokens on `body`** (custom properties resolve per element ⇒ one place covers the whole app with zero anchor coupling): the two page glass token blocks (light / dark) each append four re-declarations with the value `color-mix(in srgb, var(--we-surface-tint-light|dark) 40%, var(--dsw-static-neutral-XXX) 60%)`. **Deviation from the audit plan**: the plan's per-anchor `--dsh-scrollbar-thumb` patch was not done (all ~17 host re-declarations point at the indirect layer, so per-anchor patching could never cover them all), and `--dsh-scrollbar-width` / `-thumb-border` / `-track-margin` were left alone entirely.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-glass-surfaces.mjs` group ⑮ (all eight declarations must "exist + contain `color-mix(` + blend this theme's glass colour", with three negative controls).
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` → `npm run verify:all` exit 0 (turning the 40% weight into 0% really does go red); `lib/client.js` rebuilt in this same round. **Client-only styles ⇒ a page refresh is enough**.

- **Fix: switching the host's light / dark theme no longer re-computes the wallpaper fade background (issue [#159](https://github.com/elysia395/dsh-wallpaper-engine/issues/159) ①)**.
  **Why**: the fade background (`lastFadeBg`) was read only once while live, and nothing told it to invalidate when the host flipped `data-ds-dark-theme` ⇒ dark mode kept using the value computed for the light theme.
  **Fix**: `src/effects.js` gains `armFadeBgThemeWatch()` / `disarmFadeBgThemeWatch()` (a MutationObserver that **only watches `data-ds-dark-theme`**, idempotent, degrading silently when `MutationObserver` is missing), armed on the `wallpaperOpacity > 0` branch and torn down by the zero branch and by `clearEffects`.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-client.mjs`'s behaviour rig (cold background `#ffffff` → flipping the attribute without dispatching an event still yields `#ffffff` (negative control) → only after dispatching does it become `#000000` → the observer is gone after `clearEffects`).
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` → `npm run verify:all` exit 0; `lib/client.js` rebuilt in this same round. **Client-only ⇒ a page refresh is enough**.
  **Guard trap (for next time)**: the style text is a **template literal** baked into the artifact ⇒ a markdown backtick inside a `src/styles.js` comment makes `build-client` report 「产物语法错误：Unexpected identifier 'background'」 (hit for real this round; the same holds for the script injected by `src/effects.js`).

- **Fix: four glass surfaces had lost their backing plate (issue [#156](https://github.com/elysia395/dsh-wallpaper-engine/issues/156) ①②③④)**.
  **Why**: once the plugin replaces the page with a wallpaper, several host overlays / plates that used to lean on the page's base colour lose their visual backing — ① the host Modal's scrim paints `backdrop-filter: var(--dsw-mask-blur)` itself, but the host theme writes that token as `none` on the **bare body** and the plugin's glass page never put it back; ② the plugin manager's registry overlay (`[data-install-registry]`) has **no** `backdrop-filter` in the host source and its base token `--dsw-alias-bg-layer-2` is semi-transparent inside the plugin's gate ⇒ a pane of glass with no frost; ④ the code-block sticky bar had its backing plate wiped out together with the plugin's "clear the background" rule (the real sticky carrier is the outer `.bannerWrap`, whose plate reads `--dsw-alias-bg-base`, set to `transparent` by the plugin); ③ the composer seat plate was one the plugin had **painted extra** itself.
  **Fix**: ① re-declare `--dsw-mask-blur: blur(var(--we-blur, 16px)) saturate(var(--we-saturate, 1.8)) brightness(var(--we-glass-brightness, 1.04)) contrast(1.01)` inside the glass gate block (`src/styles.js:572`), so the host scrim paints its own frost again; ② add `body[data-we-glass-floaters] [data-install-registry] { -webkit-backdrop-filter / backdrop-filter: blur(var(--we-floaters-blur)) … }` with a no-frost-kernel fallback twin (`--we-floaters-blur` must be used **bare**, with no inner fallback — otherwise it trips `verify-glass-surfaces`'s zero-fallback consumer family); ④ re-lay `background-color` on `:has(> [data-code-block-banner])` **after** the existing clear rule, at the same specificity and with the same `!important` (the host's `MarkdownText.module.css:399` recognises that `:has`), with dark and no-frost-kernel twins; ③ **added, narrowed, then withdrawn**: the composer seat plate was first narrowed to a single bottom strip and then withdrawn entirely on the user's call — the final state paints **nothing** on `[data-composer-seat]` (that plate was only the page base colour `--dsw-alias-bg-base` that the host happened to draw, and with the page replaced by a wallpaper nothing needs it; the dock sitting straight on the wallpaper is exactly the host's native look), the `--we-composer-seat-fill` token is gone and listed in `verify-glass-surfaces`'s `REAPED_VARS`, and group ⑯ turns from a geometry ratchet into a **reverse ratchet** "the composer seat paints no plate" (any `background` / `background-image` / `background-color` / `backdrop-filter` trips it, with three old-shape negative controls); `docs/DSH-UI-INTERFACES.md`'s `data-composer-seat` ledger row is annotated "withdrawn".
  **Guards**: `test/verify-glass-surfaces.mjs` adds / hardens ① (inside the glass gate `--dsw-mask-blur` must be a real blur), ⑯ (the reverse ratchet), ⑰ (newly registered surfaces must have an **effective declaration**: anchor + gate + a real `backdrop-filter` / a real `!important` tint **in the same rule**, with two negative / positive controls) and ⑱ (whether the `--dsw-mask-blur` override is effective).
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` → `npm run verify:all` exit 0; every mutation was tried (strip #156②'s frost from the artifact, strip each of #156④'s light / dark repaints, set `--dsw-mask-blur` to `none` in the source, set the scrollbar weight to `0%` — all five mutants turn their own condition red); `lib/client.js` rebuilt in this same round. **Client-only styles ⇒ a page refresh is enough**.
  **Guard trap (for next time)**: ① groups ①/⑰ read the `CSS` **evaluated out of the artifact** (`test/verify-glass-surfaces.mjs:266` onwards), so changing only `src/styles.js` will not go red — mutation testing must change the artifact or run `npm run build` first (a false green was hit this round); ② `(?!none)` is a **false tooth**: the real file writes `backdrop-filter: none !important`, `\s*` can backtrack to nothing and the lookahead lands on the space ⇒ `none` counts as "frost that is not none", and the negative-control fixture must spell it with the space; it is now `(?![\s!]*none\b)`.

- **Fix: the host Tooltip was caught by the bubble glass (issue [#161](https://github.com/elysia395/dsh-wallpaper-engine/issues/161))**.
  **Why**: the host's bubble class name is a content hash (`_bubble_<hash>`) and the plugin's glass rules recognise bubbles by `[class*="_bubble"]`; the host's Tooltip element carries **both** `role="tooltip"` and `_bubble_<hash>` (`dsh-client-ui-primitives/lib/index.js:4841`) ⇒ it was treated as a message bubble, taking over its background and wearing a permanent frost.
  **Fix**: all four `[class*="_bubble"]` rules (the fill takeover / the always-on frost glaze / code blocks inside bubbles / the no-frost-kernel fallback that strips frost) gain `:not([role="tooltip"])`.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-readability.mjs`'s F2dN group tightens to "at least four, and every one must carry the `:not([role="tooltip"])` exclusion (a `[data-chat-flow]` scope does not substitute)", with a negative control.
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` / `verify:docs` / `npm run build` → `npm run verify:all` exit 0; `lib/client.js` rebuilt in this same round. **Client-only styles ⇒ a page refresh is enough**.

- **Typography and the glass fine-tuning rows move out of the settings page and into the sidebar's Appearance pane, both collapsed by default (second revision of ADR-0008 D4)**.
  **Why**: the settings page is a **modal dialog** — while you tune fonts or glass details it covers the page behind it, so you never see the live result of your change; and those two blocks hold too many knobs to show expanded by default without turning the sidebar's Appearance pane into clutter. ⇒ a **true migration**: the **whole Global fonts section** and the **glass advanced rows** (each surface's "independent config" layer with its parameters, plus the capsule frost / capsule colour rows under the thinking-block gate) move into the sidebar's Appearance pane, **collapsed by default**; the settings page keeps 主题 / 细节 / Glass UI (simplified config + presets) / Input caret.
  **Shape**: the font section's header becomes a clickable collapse head (`role=button` + `aria-expanded` + a rotating chevron, `we-picker__section-head--toggle`) that renders only the header while collapsed; the glass advanced rows move behind an **"Advanced glass tuning"** switch row at the **end of the Glass UI section** (built once as `advRows`; the settings page does not even build them). Every row's **intrinsic gate** (thinking-glass gear / each surface's master switch / host capability bits / follow-global) is kept exactly as it was — collapsing relocates the block wholesale and changes no row's effectiveness. **Presets go the other way and do not move**: they remain settings-page-only (a whole-snapshot overwrite with no undo does not belong in a narrow, casually reachable panel) — they are now the only settings-page-exclusive rows.
  **Expanded state**: two independent localStorage keys (`dsh-wallpaper-engine:qp-font-open` / `:qp-glass-detail`, `'1'/'0'`, **collapsed by default**), on the same "UI state only, never `config.json`" rule as `qp-tab` / `qp-view`; renderers only read `fontOpen` / `glassDetailOpen` from ctx and call `onToggleFontSection` / `onToggleGlassDetail`, so the read-only contract is unchanged. Narrow-column fallback for the sidebar: font tables get `overflow-x: auto`.
  **Two things ride along**: ① the `QP_CTX_SETTINGS_ONLY` placeholder list shrinks — 16 font fields and 10 glass-advanced fields move out (now wired with real values), leaving only `glassPresets` + the 6 playback-page fields; ② the system-font-list fetch effect moves from the settings page's `activeTab` to `QuickPanel` (`qpTab === "appearance" && fontOpen && sel.fontCustom`) — the settings Appearance tab no longer hosts any font UI, so fetching there paid the host scan (measured ~10s on macOS) for nothing. The bottom-bar deep link label changes from 「字体与更多外观 ›」 to 「更多外观设置 ›」.
  **Guards**: `verify-scene-live` flips the font-section gate (`gatedSidebar` + negative control) and adds a fourth `MORE_CASES` set (settings = four sections and never any independent config / sidebar collapsed by default / sidebar with glass expanded / sidebar with fonts expanded); the placeholder teeth now pin `onFpsCap`/`glassPresets` and **reversely** assert that the font and glass-advanced fields are **gone** from the list; the FREE table gains 17 font identifiers plus `ensureSystemFonts`. `verify-fontset`'s surface judgments **flip** (settings has no 全局字体, the sidebar does) and rig ⑧ renders with `surface:'sidebar'`+`fontOpen:true`; `verify-client`'s behavior block is split across two render trees (settings rig = simplified rows + migration negative assertions; sidebar rig `renderSidePane` = advanced round-trips + the font switch — its harness gains a `ctx.get` stub so `installSidebarRight` really registers the sidebar body); `verify-system-fonts`' trigger-source condition and dropdown rig follow the move; `verify-presets` gains a sidebar-only handler exemption (`onToggleGlassDetail`); `fontset-load-smoke`'s mount gets the same `ctx.get` stub and qp-* seeding; `guard-map` recomputed (`verify-system-fonts` now also watches `src/quick-panel.js`).
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` / `verify:docs` / `smoke` / `npm run build` all exit 0; `lib/client.js` rebuilt in this same commit. **Client-only UI layout change ⇒ a page refresh is enough** (no new settings keys, `config.json` untouched).

- **Three wallpaper-layer framing sliders under Playback → Effects: 水平 / 垂直 / 缩放 (horizontal / vertical / scale)** (community PR [#155](https://github.com/elysia395/dsh-wallpaper-engine/pull/155) · wwexplorer); the value box on those three rows is now **typeable**, and **double-clicking the label** puts the row back to its default.
  **Why**: the horizontal / vertical / scale group behind "alignment = free" in Wallpaper Engine's own property panel (`alignmentx` / `alignmenty` / `alignmentz`) is a WE engine property stored in WE's `config.json` (`<user>.wproperties.<wallpaper path>.MonitorN.*`), **not** in `project.json`; the plugin's 壁纸属性 panel only reads the `general.properties` the author defined in the editor, and scene fit offers only cover / contain / center / fill (`SCENE_LIVE_FIT`) ⇒ bringing WE's framing over means setting it again here. The three ranges deliberately **match WE's same-named sliders** so values can be copied across: position 0..100 (50 = centred, 0 / 100 = either end), scale 50..150 (100 = original size, 95 = shrunk to 95%, revealing the page base colour around the edges).
  **Fix**: new settings keys `layerPositionX` / `layerPositionY` / `layerScale` (the single source of truth is still `lib/settings-schema.js`, whose table both ends' sanitize / serialize walk — miss one end and the value is permanently `undefined` in the selection, with no value on the slider and nothing written while dragging). The values land on the `.we-layer` element's **standalone** `translate` / `scale` properties (**not `transform`**: the layer-switch transition and `--we-layer--repaint` both touch `transform`, and sharing it would have them clear each other), written as CSS variables by `applyEffects` in `src/effects.js` and removed in pairs by `clearEffects`; **an at-default value writes no variable** (otherwise it forces a permanent compositing layer). The three rows sit after 饱和度 and before 壁纸透明度, and the sidebar's Playback pane draws the same batch (through `qpRenderPlaybackPane`, which must receive the **real handlers** — ⚠️ never just register them in `QP_CTX_SETTINGS_ONLY`: that list's object is `Object.assign`'s **first** argument and would stamp a "throw on access" stand-in over the **settings page**'s tree as well). The value area gains `opts.numberEdit`: the right-hand readout becomes a `NumberValueInput` that is **uncontrolled while editing** (local draft state, committed and clamped on Enter / blur, emptied = the edit is abandoned) — a controlled input locks itself up on "clear and retype"; `opts.onLabelDoubleClick` resets on a label double-click (position → 50, scale → 100%).
  **Guards**: two new conditions in `test/verify-client.mjs` (the uncontrolled shape of `NumberValueInput`, plus a negative control forbidding a controlled `onInput(Number(raw), true)` per key); `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` adds the four handler names to the sidebar free-variable table and inserts 水平 / 垂直 / 缩放 after 饱和度 in five slider-label-sequence fixtures; `test/fixtures/settings-sanitize-golden.json` gains the three keys.
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` (38) / `verify:docs` / `npm run build` / `smoke` exit 0; `lib/client.js` rebuilt in this same commit. **Both the client and the host-side settings table changed ⇒ restart `dsh web`** (both ends have to know these three keys).

- **Fix: with "conversation avatars" enabled, a collapsed reasoning row disappeared together with its own toggle (issue #154: "thinking is simply invisible / after collapsing it by hand there is no way to find it again")**.
  **Root cause**: the avatar decorator turns three kinds of message row (`data-chat-flow-kind` = user / steering / assistant-step) into a horizontal flex container and gives "everything that is not the avatar" `flex: 1 1 auto; min-width: 0` so it fills the remaining width. But the host wraps node rendering in a **slot outlet anchor** (`div[data-slot="conversation.chat.node"]`, to which the host renderer writes inline `style="display: contents"` — its own comment says this lets flex/grid parents "see the slot's own children") — that layer has **no box**, so it is neither a flex item nor subject to flex properties ⇒ the rule **hits thin air**. The real flex item is the anchor's child (an assistant message's content root `div.v5IAXa_root`), which falls back to the default `flex: 0 1 auto` and therefore sizes along the main axis by its own **max-content** width instead of filling the row. Meanwhile the host hard-codes `contain: size layout` on a **collapsed** reasoning row (`._3GBCTG_root:not([data-expanded])`; `data-expanded` is only written while expanded) — size containment zeroes its intrinsic size ⇒ when the only visible content of a row is the collapsed "thinking" row, its max-content is 0 ⇒ that flex item is **0px wide** and the whole message, toggle included, is invisible. Expanding removes the matching `contain:size` rule ⇒ intrinsic width returns ⇒ visible; collapsing again zeroes it ⇒ "no way to find it again". A reasoning-only row (`data-chat-group-part="reasoning"`, whose flow-kind is also `assistant-step`, so it is decorated too) stays in that shape permanently and is the most reliable reproduction.
  **Fix (styles only; neither the decorator nor the host contract is touched)**: the content-cell rule becomes **two selectors, one declaration block** — content attached directly, plus the layer behind the slot outlet anchor: `body[data-we-avatar="on"] [data-we-avatar-row] > :not(.we-avatar), body[data-we-avatar="on"] [data-we-avatar-row] > [data-slot] > :not(.we-avatar) { flex: 1 1 auto; min-width: 0; }` (`flex-grow: 1` is the load-bearing part: content whose intrinsic width is 0 still fills the row only by growing). The anchor itself is **not** given `display: block` — the host contract relies on `display:contents` so parents see the slot's children, and changing it would skew the host's own column-gap and empty-item rules.
  **Guards**: two tightenings in the avatar section of `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` — (a) the CSS pin becomes a two-selector regex applied to the **comment-stripped** stylesheet (reverting to the old single-selector form fails immediately) plus a negative control ("never write `display:` for `[data-slot]`"), and its discriminating power was verified against `git show HEAD:src/styles.js` and two deliberate mutations (a non-vacuous pin); (b) the fake-DOM row is now built in the **host's real shape**: content wrapped in `div[data-slot="conversation.chat.node"]` (`style="display: contents"`), asserting that the avatar is inserted **before the anchor** (row = [avatar, anchor]), that the anchor holds only the content root (the decorator inserts nothing inside it) and that turning the switch off restores the row byte-for-byte (only the anchor left, content root object not rebuilt). The previous fake DOM hung content directly off the row, which is exactly what hid this bug (elsewhere in the repo fake elements are already built in the host's real shape; the avatar section missed it).
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` / `verify:docs` / `npm run build` exit 0; `lib/client.js` rebuilt (the new selector sits on its lines 4394–4395). Only the client render output changed ⇒ a page refresh is enough, no `dsh web` restart.
  **Guard trap (for next time)**: the whole stylesheet is embedded in the build output as a **template string**, so a single backtick inside a `src/styles.js` comment makes `build-client` fail with "product syntax error: Unexpected identifier …" (hit for real this round, by adding backticks around `div[data-slot=…]` in a comment).

- **Video playback no longer waits for the whole file to be read: `faststart` changed from "write a full copy" to a "virtual layout composed at serve time" (disk usage 1570 MB → 0, and this path no longer needs ffmpeg at all)**.
  **Root cause**: Chromium's media loader reads the request it issues itself (`Range: bytes=0-`) **straight through**, and these wallpaper sources keep `moov` (duration / index tables) at the **end of the file** ⇒ without moov there is no duration and no playback, so time-to-first-frame is proportional to **file size**. Measured on this machine (`E:\SteamLibrary\...\431960`, ≈430 MB/s): iris2 `764,688,296 B` waits **1761 ms**, lv_0 `501,752,315 B` waits **1250 ms**, kei `155,604,213 B` waits 357 ms, 梓 (ahq) `101,749,329 B` waits 324 ms; the same iris2 with moov already up front takes **149–233 ms**. The old implementation wrote a **full copy** `faststart/fs_<hash>.mp4` via ffmpeg `-c copy -movflags +faststart` — measured **1570.28 MB across 5 files** (each a complete copy of its source), and its cleanup was an 8 GB LRU cap ⇒ those copies were **never** evicted.
  **Fix (host, read-only + composed at serve time)**: new `lib/mp4-vfs.js` derives an equivalent layout by reading the source only: ① the top-level box table (seeking to each box header as needed); ② `moov` read into memory whole; ③ `moov` moved ahead of `mdat` with **every** chunk offset in `stco`/`co64` shifted by `+len(moov)`; ④ a **segment table** (`[boxes before mdat as-is][patched moov][mdat and after]`). `lib/serve.js` gains `serveLayout` / `layoutRangeStream`, which slice a Range across that table (single in-memory segment / single file segment / spanning), with **Range / 206 / 416 / HEAD / suffix semantics identical to `serveFile`**. The key property: the **virtual file is exactly as long as the source** ⇒ `Content-Length` and the Range endpoints are bit-for-bit unchanged and neither the client nor `probeMp4` has to know; `moov` (16–188 KB each) lives only in memory, so this costs **0 bytes of disk**. `/media` choice still has to go through `pinnedFaststartVariant` (the two layouts have different byte offsets, so one playback must never read the original for the first half and the virtual layout for the second ⇒ garbage), which now pins "virtual layout or original for this playback".
  **Anything it cannot judge falls back to the original file, quietly** (this is an optimisation, not a feature): not mp4/m4v/mov, `moov` already first, no `mdat` or several, any offset outside `mdat`, fragment/auxiliary markers in `moov` (`moof`/`mvex`/`traf`/`saio`/`senc`…), no `stco`/`co64`, `moov` > 8 MiB, IO failure — all serve the original exactly as before. The `faststart/fs_*.mp4` copies left behind by earlier versions (including atomic-write `.tmp` leftovers) are reclaimed once at startup by `sweepLegacyFaststartVariants()` (capped at 512 entries, only names matching `fs_*.mp4`).
  **Equivalence evidence (5 real sources, `ffmpeg 6.0`)**: all five are judged virtual, the bytes rebuilt from the segment table are **byte-identical** to an independently computed faststart layout, `ffmpeg -v error -f null -` prints **nothing**, and `framemd5` is **frame-for-frame identical for video and audio** (梓 812/636 lines · iris2 3895/1406 · lv_0 5581/4009 · alice 6810/5323 · kei 7010/5479); against ffmpeg's own `+faststart` output the sizes differ by −35 B / +537 B / −55220 B / +1 B / 0 B (a **non-vacuous control**: the virtual layout does not re-order the `free` box). The `faststart` directory on this machine went from 1570.28 MB to **0** (moov totals ≈0.5 MB in memory).
  **Guards**: new `test/verify-mp4-vfs.mjs` (**43 checks**, fixtures **hand-built in pure JS**, no ffmpeg needed — the correctness of this chain is just "box table + offset shift" arithmetic): 16 layout-maths checks (including `co64` wide tables, `moov` already first ⇒ `plain`, and the individual reason for two `mdat`s / no `moov` / offsets out of range / truncated top-level boxes / fragment markers / missing `stbl` / oversized `moov` / unknown extension), the analyser's read-only and caching behaviour (same source twice ⇒ same verdict, an undecidable verdict is **not** cached, a changed source is re-analysed, analysis does not alter one byte of the source), an end-to-end run of the real `apply()` against `/media` (206 + `Content-Range`/`Content-Length` from the virtual length; the body equals the independently computed virtual bytes **and differs from the on-disk original**; a slice spanning the `moov`/`mdat` boundary; suffix; HEAD; 416; a second request returning the same bytes; a source that cannot be judged served as the original; `/preview` not involved; **no `faststart` directory under the cache root afterwards**), the legacy sweep (three files matching the pattern removed, `preview.mp4`/`notes.txt` untouched), and 7 checks for the read-only transcode-cache answer of `/media-info` (see the next entry). The ① ratchet in `verify-scene-live` now spans **four files** with positive and negative controls (`lib/faststart.js` pinning / `lib/mp4-vfs.js` maths / `lib/serve.js` segment mapping / `lib/routes/media-bytes.js` consumer) and puts the entire "copy to disk + run ffmpeg" path **in the negative control**. Wired into `npm run verify` (37 → **38** entries).
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` (38 entries) / `verify:docs` exit 0; `docs/ROUTE-INDEX.md` and `docs/GUARD-MAP.md` were regenerated (`lib/**` line numbers moved, one new module and one new guard). **Both the host and the client half changed ⇒ restart `dsh web` and rebuild `lib/client.js`** (rebuilt in this round).
  **Guard trap (for next time)**: under the sandbox `spawnSync(ffmpeg)` **always hits EPERM on piped stdio** (`stdout`/`stderr` both `undefined`) — comparing two `stdout` readings directly makes "framemd5 identical" **falsely green**; for evidence, write output to files with `stdio: ['ignore', fd, fd]` and **assert the output is non-empty**. (Likewise: ffmpeg's `-map` must come after `-i`, and never write its output next to the sources in a Steam library.)

- **Play a cached frame-capped version directly: no more "fetch the original first, then swap once the probe comes back"**.
  **Root cause**: `/media-info` (the probe taken when a wallpaper is selected) only answered with source metadata, so the client could not know that **the frame-capped version for the current cap was already on disk** ⇒ the layer **started on the original**, then `maybeUpgradeToTranscoded` (`src/video-layer.js`) probed `/transcoded` with a 1-byte `Range` and only swapped once that came back; and if the layer was already on screen the swap was deferred to the next build (swapping clears the frame already presented — the real-machine "solid colour frame" root cause, see `layerStillPending`) ⇒ the first selection always fetched the original for nothing.
  **Fix**: `/media-info?fps=<current cap>` now answers `transcode: { fps, cached }`, where `cached` comes **only** from `transcodeCached` (one `existsSync`) and **shares `transcodeCacheKey(abs, mtimeMs, fps)` with the file `/transcoded` writes** — computing the key twice drifts into "it says cached but the request misses"; it **never starts a transcode** (running ffmpeg once per selected wallpaper is a waste this route has actually produced). No `fps` parameter / a cap ≤ 0 ⇒ `transcode: null`. The client's `refreshMediaInfo` now asks with the current cap and, on a hit where the source really is above the cap, records that capped copy in `selection.transcodeReady` ⇒ **the next layer build uses it as `src`** (the existing `useCached` branch of `buildVideoMedia`); it still **does not** touch `<video>` from the probe — when the change lands is decided only by the layer build and `maybeUpgradeToTranscoded`. **Both the host and the client changed ⇒ rebuilding `lib/client.js` is now part of changing the client** (done in this round).
  **Guards**: `test/verify-transcode-state.mjs` +6 checks (one key function, the read-only wiring, no transcode call anywhere in the `/media-info` handler body with a negative control, the client sending `?fps=`, the `transcodeReady` write site, and a single `transcodedUrlFor` URL builder with a negative control); `test/verify-mp4-vfs.mjs` gained a **7-check section E** (no `fps` ⇒ `null`; nothing cached ⇒ `cached:false` **and no transcode directory created**; a file placed at the path **the guard computes independently** from `sha256(abs|round(mtime)|fps)` ⇒ `cached:true`; cap 0 ⇒ `null`; another cap may not borrow the cache; metadata and the cache answer arrive in one probe). The guard went from 36 to **43 checks**.

- **Fix: Wallpaper Engine installation detection failed — the current release put the executables inside a `distribution/` subdirectory (reported downstream: an install on a non-default Steam drive was judged "not installed")**.
  **Root cause**: the only "is it installed" test in `locateWallpaperEngineP()` was the presence of `<dir>\wallpaper32.exe` at the top level. The current WE release leaves only `ChromaAppInfo.xml` / `installer.exe` / `launcher.exe` at the top and moves `wallpaper32.exe` / `wallpaper64.exe` down into `distribution\` (next to a stable `version.json`) ⇒ an installed copy was judged not installed. **This is not a degradation but a disappearance of functionality**: portable projects (`<install root>/projects/defaultprojects|myprojects`) were skipped wholesale and the WE playlists became unreadable (`readPlaylistsP` needs `<install root>/config.json`) ⇒ rotation / playlists broke. (The report's "the Workshop scan is skipped entirely" is *not* this bug's mechanism: `libraryDirs` → `<library>/steamapps/workshop/content/431960` never went through installDir.) The real install on this machine (`E:\SteamLibrary`) happens to be the **classic layout** (top-level `wallpaper32.exe`) — so the classic test has to stay.
  **Fix (host, all in `lib/index.js`)**: new `WE_INSTALL_MARKERS` (top-level `wallpaper32.exe` / `wallpaper64.exe` plus `distribution/`'s `wallpaper32.exe` / `wallpaper64.exe` / `version.json`) and `isWallpaperEngineRootP(dir)` (probing each marker) — **both layouts are accepted** (the official CLI documentation still describes only the classic layout, so that test is not dropped; `version.json` is the stable marker in case the executables are renamed again); `locateWallpaperEngineP()` now uses it. **It always returns the installation root itself** (where `projects/` and `config.json` live), never `distribution\` — consumers need the root, not the directory holding the executable.
  Two shapes on the same chain that made "the user set it and it still did nothing" are fixed along the way: ① `owningLibrariesP()` now probes **the directory that is actually scanned** (`<root>/steamapps/workshop/content/431960`) instead of the `steamapps/common/wallpaper_engine` **directory name** — an uninstall leftover shell is no longer taken for a library (this machine has one holding only `ui/` and `log.txt`), and a library whose install folder was renamed / is absent but whose Workshop content is there is no longer missed; ② `steamProbeDirsP()` now puts the **`DSH_WE_STEAM_ROOT` override ahead of the registry and the common directories** (`locate` returns the **first** hit, so with the registry first an explicit override could never steer away from a Steam-known install, and fixture-based self-checks would silently read the developer's real install) and lets **the env value participate in the 60s probe cache key** (otherwise a changed override is masked by the previous probe's TTL).
  **Guards**: new `test/verify-we-install-probe.mjs` (**20 checks**) — the behavioural half drives the real host through `GET /wallpaper-engine/inventory` with four synthetic Steam roots (classic / modern / stale / Workshop-content-only), each with a fresh `apply()`: the `distribution/` layout must be recognised (the old test returns null here = this issue's regression gate) with installDir being the root and not `distribution\`, the portable project must be scanned and the WE playlist resolved to a wallpaper id; the classic layout must not be dropped; an uninstall leftover shell is not an install (a real install on the developer's machine is tolerated by design); a root holding only Workshop content is still a wallpaper source. The fixtures carry their own shape negative control (`modern` genuinely has **no** executable at the top — otherwise group B would be falsely green); a separate set of source ratchets with negative controls pins the marker table, `isWallpaperEngineRootP`, the `'workshop', 'content', WE_APPID` probe, the probe order and the cache key. Wired into `npm run verify` (36 → **37** entries).
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` (37 entries) / `verify:docs` exit 0; `docs/ROUTE-INDEX.md` and `docs/GUARD-MAP.md` were regenerated (host line numbers moved). **The host half changed ⇒ restart `dsh web`** (no client change this time, so `lib/client.js` needs no rebuild).
  **Guard trap (for next time)**: `DSH_WE_STEAM_ROOT` means the **Steam root** (the level containing `steamapps/`), not the WE install folder — passing the install folder makes candidates become `<…>/wallpaper_engine/steamapps/common/wallpaper_engine` and **miss everything** (hit while writing the guard: all four cases fell through to the real install on the developer's machine, which looks like "the env override is broken").

- **New "cache location" setting: move the multi-GB cache off the system drive (user feedback: the Windows "C-drive cleanliness" complaint); plus two hard-coded paths that bypassed the data directory**. Scope decision: **make the cache movable and close the coverage gaps only** — the data directory itself stays put, because relocating it into `$DSH_HOME` merely folds the scattered folders into DSH's single root, which still defaults to the same system drive and does not solve the disk-space complaint.
  **Root cause**: the cache root could previously only be changed through the `DSH_WE_CACHE_DIR` environment variable — **undiscoverable and non-persistable** for ordinary users; the measured `~/.dsh-wallpaper-engine` on this machine is ≈ **4.94 GB**, of which `cache` alone is **4.84 GB** (transcodes 2.86 / faststart 1.57 / frames 0.41), the single real pain point (settings, avatars and fonts together are 0 MB, so moving those would achieve nothing). Two places also **bypassed `pluginDataDir()`**: `DEFAULT_UPLOAD_DIR = join(homedir(), '.dsh-wallpaper-engine', 'uploads')` and the same shape in `customFrameDir()` ⇒ setting `DSH_WE_DATA_DIR` could not move either one (`DSH_WE_UPLOAD_DIR` covered only the first).
  **Fix (host)**: new `lib/routes/cache-dir.js` (`GET` returns `{ dir, effective, same }`; `POST { dir, migrate }` resolves → validates → migrates → persists to config; a relative path or a target occupied by a file ⇒ 400) and a `cacheBaseDir()` **accessor** (chain: `DSH_WE_CACHE_DIR` → the `cacheDir` root field in `config.json` → the `<data dir>/cache` default, re-resolved **on every call, never a value snapshot** — same reasoning as `getUploadDir()`); `effective` is the path **actually in effect**, which is how the UI honestly shows "overridden by the environment". All six cache subdirectories (transcodes / faststart / frames / video-previews / media-bridge / artwork) now go through `join(cacheBaseDir(), <n>)`. The media-bridge and artwork leg initially passed the cache root **into the factory as a value** (a snapshot taken at startup), so after a change new artwork / bridge caches would drift back to the old drive; it now passes the **accessor and re-resolves on every use** (`now-playing.js` hands `cacheBaseDir` itself to `createMediaBackend`, and both factories funnel through a local `cacheRoot()`, leaving string call sites untouched — the same reasoning as `cacheBaseDir()` never caching a snapshot), pinned by guards D3/D3b with a negative control.; migration reuses the upload-directory `moveFileP` (rename hitting EXDEV ⇒ copy + unlink, so it **works across drives**), moving **only the plugin's own cache subdirectories** — unrecognised entries stay put and the old directory is left behind as empty shells, **never deleted**; both hard-coded gaps now resolve through `pluginDataDir()` (the `DEFAULT_UPLOAD_DIR` constant is gone, replaced by `defaultUploadDir()`).
  **Fix (client)**: a new **cache location** section under the **Advanced** tab (path in effect / change / text input / migration result "migrated N cache files, M skipped"), placed **before the live-render diagnostics section** (settings rows come before troubleshooting toggles); 9 new i18n entries; the `Inventory` payload gains a `cacheDir` field (with its `.d.ts` declaration).
  **Guards**: new `test/verify-cache-dir.mjs` (**31 checks**: endpoint shape — GET 405 / relative path 400 / target is a file 400; resolution chain with positive/negative controls — env override, short-circuit when pointed back at the current directory without rewriting, the default still answerable after deleting `config.json` **without recreating it**, and reconciliation with `/inventory`; migration of all six subdirectories plus "unrecognised entries stay put" and `migrate:false` changing the root only; a derivation ratchet plus **negative controls** for the two old shapes, with zero hits across `lib/**/*.js`), wired into `npm run verify` (35 → **36** entries); the section-order behavioural guard in `verify-scene-live` gains "cache location"; the pinned Inventory field table in `verify-types` gains `cacheDir`.
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` (36 entries) / `verify:docs` / `npm run build` all exit 0. **The host half changed ⇒ restart `dsh web`** (the client half needs only a page refresh).
  **Guard trap (for next time)**: `/inventory` has a 3s TTL (`INVENTORY_TTL_MS = 3000` in `lib/inventory.js`) ⇒ judging "the change took effect immediately" from the UI reading returns the **previous** state; a guard must wait out the TTL or ask the TTL-free `effective` instead.

- **Refactor (no behaviour change): host god-function and client deep-nesting cleanup + two redundancy merges** (move-first: change *where* a declaration lives, then change what it does in a separate pass).
  **Scale**: `apply()` 1541 → 333 lines (`lib/index.js` 4411 → 2955, four host factories + seven route families extracted); `WallpaperPicker()` 796 → 364 lines;
  `syncLayers()` 276 → 169; `renderPickerModal()` 311 → 63; `QuickPanel()` 431 → 135; deep nesting (indent ≥22) in `picker-modal` 77 → 3 lines and `quick-panel` 59 → 2 lines.
  **Deduplication**: host JSON-response boilerplate 10 → 1 (`lib/json-response.js`; 16 call sites across 11 files keep their local alias, behaviour preserved verbatim — two endpoints never sent `Cache-Control`, kept via the 4th argument `null`);
  client clamp / monotonic clock / body-root copies 7 → 1 (`src/we-base.js`'s `weClampTo` / `weNow` / `weBody`; deliberately *not* merged with the settings-side `clampNum`, which has the opposite semantics).
  **Comments & docs**: dated comments 50 → 0 in `src/`, 12 → 0 in `lib/`; chronicle framing 57 → 9 and 14 → 1; drift-prone version numbers / counts in evergreen docs replaced by symbol references.
  **Guards**: new `verify-json-response` (sole implementation + call-site ratchet + 6 positive/negative controls); two-way assertions (with negative controls) added to `verify-client` / `verify-scene-live` and friends.
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` (35 guards) / `verify:docs` / `smoke` (6 suites) exit 0; rebuilt `lib/client.js` is byte-identical to the committed artifact. No new dependencies, no setting or protocol changes.

- **The Wallpaper Engine entry icon on the sidebar start page (guide) now uses the same fixed-palette color as the official artwork family** (user request: "make the wallpaper engine icon a colored icon consistent with the other features").
  **Root cause**: every official guide icon is **fixed-palette** artwork — Files = amber `#FFBC4D` fill, Terminal = `#679EFE` stroke, Browser = `#539CFA` stroke (36-unit grid); our React-side icon used `currentColor`, which resolves to the guide container's gray ink (`--dsw-alias-label-secondary`) ⇒ the only gray entry on the page.
  **Fix**: the React face of the icon in `src/nav-icon.js` (the face the guide card consumes) now uses the fixed family color `#A797FC` (the purple from the official `PluginArtworkLoop`, keeping the hue distinct from the amber and blue neighbors); geometry and stroke width unchanged (≈2.3px at 26px, right between Terminal's 2.5px and Browser's 1.4px). The settings-nav DOM patch face (`weIconSvgString`) **keeps `currentColor`** — the official settings nav is a monochrome line-icon family by design.
  **Verification**: `npm run verify` all green (the `verify-i18n` nav-icon anchor guards pass unchanged); eyeballed a render-rig mock (light/dark × 26/22px): the purple "frame + sun + mountains" reads clearly in both themes with a hue distinct from the neighbors. **Client-only change ⇒ a page reload is enough** (⚠️ but see the erratum below: swapping the bundle mid-session on the official desktop can fail to fetch — a cold restart is the reliable path).

- **Fix: the native select dropdown popup rendered option rows with background and text in the same color — the whole list unreadable** (user report + screenshot: in the dark theme the popup showed a white canvas with near-invisible light text).
  **Root cause**: Chromium's dropdown popup is a **separate document** — its canvas takes the `<select>`'s own background-color only when that background is **opaque**, and our selects are transparent glass (`background: transparent`, so the glass panel shows the wallpaper) ⇒ the popup falls back to its default **light** canvas; the option text, however, inherits the select's glass ink `--we-ink` (in the dark theme = the host's light label token) ⇒ light text on a light canvas. Every dropdown inside the settings window (transition / rotation interval / content rating / type / audio source / …) was hit; the drawer and official right-rail selects survived only because the host paints them with an opaque background — which is exactly the "almost all dropdowns but not quite every one" shape.
  **Fix**: `src/styles.js` gives the option rows an explicit opaque panel background plus the glass ink (`.we-picker select option, .we-picker__select option { background-color: var(--we-panel-color, #ffffff); color: var(--we-ink, #1f2328); }`) — Chromium paints the popup row by row from each option's **resolved** computed style (vars resolve in-page; the popup document copies the results), and both vars flip with the light/dark theme, so both themes read correctly. The class-based scope (`.we-picker__select`) deliberately covers the sidebar selects that don't live under a `.we-picker` ancestor.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-readability.mjs` gains **F8** — the option rule must exist (matched by selector after comment-stripping), must declare both the `--we-panel-color` background and the `--we-ink` ink, with two negative controls (background reverted to translucent glass / ink declaration dropped must both be caught). `lib/client.js` rebuilt, `npm run verify` / `verify:docs` / `smoke` all exit 0. Verified in a real-Chromium render rig: light theme option = white bg + dark ink, dark theme option = `#1e1f26` opaque bg + ink (on the real host the ink resolves to the host label token #ffffff, ≈15.9:1 against the #232324 background). **Client-only change ⇒ takes effect after a cold restart.**

- **Fix: on the official desktop (0.2.0-rc.x, dockkit) the conversation column punched through the glass right sidebar in the sidebar's fullscreen mode** ([#150](https://github.com/elysia395/dsh-wallpaper-engine/issues/150), reported by Citrus-Cat, reproduced locally on 0.2.0-rc.2; triggers with the plugin at factory defaults).
  **Symptom**: collapsed and half-screen (push) modes are fine; only in fullscreen the conversation's hero headline and composer card render on top of the sidebar content. Natively (no plugin) fullscreen shows the sidebar alone over the whole workspace.
  **Root cause**: on 0.2.0-rc.x the dockkit elevation system gives the dock cell **inside** the panel its z-index by consuming `--dsh-dockkit-dock-layer` (10 normally / 40 in right-rail fullscreen), which is what covers the conversation column (whose top content is z=9). Our right-rail glass applies `backdrop-filter` to the **panel element itself** ⇒ the panel becomes a stacking context (z=auto tier), trapping that z inside the panel; the panel as an atomic unit falls back to z=auto and gets over-painted by the conversation's higher-layer flex items (hero composer z=1 / composerSeat z=7, same root stacking context). Push and collapsed modes have no spatial overlap so nothing shows; only fullscreen puts the panel and the conversation on top of each other. Local evidence chain: removing the plugin stylesheet ⇒ stacking instantly correct again (panel content back on top); diffing the stacking-context chains of both subtrees ⇒ the only delta is the panel's own backdrop-filter; inlining `z-index: var(--dsh-dockkit-dock-layer, 10)` on the panel (resolves to 40 in fullscreen) ⇒ overlap gone.
  **Fix**: `src/styles.js`'s right-rail glass rule (the one declaring backdrop-filter) gains `z-index: var(--dsh-dockkit-dock-layer, 10)` — lifting the panel to exactly the layer the host designed it for (the var is declared on the panel itself and resolves to 40 in fullscreen, the same layer the inner dock cell gets natively). The master-switch-off fallback (opaque background only) and the software-raster fallback (backdrop-filter explicitly none) create no stacking context and keep the native paint order — deliberately untouched.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-host-paint-scope.mjs` gains **H4** — any rule declaring a blur-type backdrop-filter on the panel must also declare `z-index: var(--dsh-dockkit-dock-layer…)` in the same rule, with two negative controls (stripping the lift from the real rule / a synthetic blur-without-lift rule must both be caught); H0–H3 pass unchanged. `lib/client.js` rebuilt, `npm run verify` / `verify:docs` / `smoke` all exit 0. Live re-verified on 0.2.0-rc.2 after a cold restart: fullscreen panel computed z-index=40, the glass blur intact, and elementFromPoint at the hero returns panel content again (the conversation SPAN before the fix), screenshot `.test-cache/issue150/issue150-fixed.png`. **Client-only change ⇒ a page reload is enough** (⚠️ but see the next entry: swapping client.js mid-session is its own trap).

- **⚠️ Verification-method correction: after rebuilding lib/client.js while the official desktop is running, a page reload alone can fail the fetch (`net::ERR_ABORTED`) — restart the host** (observed during the #150 verification).
  **Symptom**: rebuilding the client bundle mid-session (content changed) ⇒ after a page reload the plugin import never settles, the boot screen sticks at "Loading plugins…", and the host log reports `web boot: 1 entry did not activate` + `import failed (see console for the import error)`; the CDP network log shows **only this plugin's** `plugins/??dsh-plugin-wallpaper-engine/client.js&rev=…` being aborted (every other plugin finishes; another module in the same batch was also aborted once and succeeded on a retry with a different rev). Rolling the bundle back to bytes identical to the cold-start build still failed ⇒ content-independent: the host wedges its rev/relay state when the file changes under it. The earlier art-gate experiment's "checkout + reload picks it up" conclusion is not reliable for this repo's current cadence (multiple rebuilds per session).
  **Method**: after touching the client bundle, always verify with a **cold restart** (`taskkill //F //IM "DeepSeek Harness.exe"`, then relaunch); don't gamble on reload.

- **Sidebar top bar gains a Refresh button next to Pause + update-notice rework: smaller art and a 16pt shout-out that the sidebar is only the quick version** (user asks, verbatim: ①「在暂停的旁边添加一个刷新壁纸仓库的按钮，因为总有用户找不到设置页面的刷新键」— add a library-refresh button next to Pause because users keep failing to find the refresh key on the settings page; ②「缩小一些更新公告中的图片」— shrink the notice art a bit; ③「用 word 文档中的 16 号大小的字体向用户说明侧边栏的调节只是简略版，细致的调节在设置页的壁纸引擎页中，可以用夸张一点的表达效果因为用户总是忽视我的更新公告」— use Word's 16pt font size to tell users the sidebar controls are only the quick version and fine-grained tuning lives on the settings page's Wallpaper Engine tab, exaggeration allowed because users keep ignoring the notice).
  **What**: ① the current-wallpaper row's button cluster in the sidebar grows from Pause / Clear to **Pause / Refresh / Clear** (`src/quick-panel.js`) — the **same action** as the settings page's Refresh (`loadInventory()`), the same in-flight state (`Refreshing…` + disabled), with a tooltip spelling out "Rescan the Wallpaper Engine library (newly installed or removed wallpapers show up right away)"; the drawer shell and the official right-sidebar tab share this one markup. ② the notice art's `max-height` drops from 38vh to **26vh** (`src/styles.js`; ≈410px → 280px at 1080p, so the square art no longer owns half the screen). ③ a new large-type paragraph under the notice's 💡 tips (`we-update-notice__callout`, **`font-size: 16pt` ≈ 21px, about twice the body** — pt is an absolute unit and ignores the body's 0.82em scaling): "❗❗❗ **侧边栏的调节只是「简略版」！**细致的调节都在「设置 → 壁纸引擎」里！" (The sidebar controls are only the quick version! Fine-grained tuning lives in Settings → Wallpaper Engine!) — the exaggeration is the font size and the exclamation marks, nothing factual. **`NOTICE_VERSION` moves to `1.3.1`**: without bumping the sentinel, everyone who dismissed the r2 notice would never see the new line (the edit would be a no-op), so it re-shows once.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-i18n.mjs` bidirectional reconciliation (3 new keys: one tooltip + the two callout sentences, zh/en in sync); `verify-about` §5 art-gate assertions pass unchanged (the gating structure is untouched); `lib/client.js` rebuilt, `npm run verify` / `verify:docs` / `smoke` all exit 0. Eyeballed in a real-browser render rig: the three top-bar buttons fit on one row in the 360px drawer without wrapping, the title truncates normally; the 16pt callout and the smaller art look as intended. **Client-only change ⇒ a page reload is enough.**

### v1.3.0-r2 (unreleased)

> Increments after v1.3.0 (three things fixed after the GitHub Release v1.3.0 asset went out and before
> the npm channel publish); `package.json`'s `version` moves to `1.3.0-r2` in step.

- **Fix: after switching sessions (or re-applying the same wallpaper in settings) the wallpaper kept playing but the mouse silently stopped working — the live-active flag was cleared to false by a same-id re-selection and nothing ever set it back** (upstream issue, with the local heartbeat `playing=true` + `watch=… first=1` + `liveOn=0` coexisting as direct evidence).
  **Symptom**: after switching sessions or re-selecting the same wallpaper, Scene pointer parallax and click interaction plus every pointer effect inside Web wallpapers go dead, and only a full page reload brings them back; the picture keeps playing (`we-live-on` and the underlay image were already at their final state), so users only ever see "the wallpaper is broken sometimes".
  **Root cause**: the normal path of `applySelection()` in `src/media-prep.js` clears `selection.sceneLiveActive` **unconditionally**, while that flag is both the runtime state of "the render page is alive" and the downstream gate; on a same-id re-selection the layer key (`wantKey`, no version or timestamp in it) is byte-identical ⇒ the `syncLayers()` path in `src/live-layer.js` takes the `adopt-live` branch: the render page is not reloaded and the first-frame gate is not walked again. The **only** place in the whole file that sets it true is that first-frame gate (walked once in a lifetime) ⇒ the flag stays false forever, and every consumer is an early return: `livePointerFlush` / `livePointerSample` block pointer injection (the window-level capture listeners are in fact still attached), and the 1 s beat in `startMediaSync` blocks the audio spectrum and Now Playing forwarding.
  **Fix (all three together — ① root cause / ② immediate recovery / ③ self-healing backstop)**: ① the clearing site is guarded by `idChanged` (comparing the old and new id computed **before** `selection.id` is assigned, sharing that one comparison with the neighbouring `cancelLiveMount("selection")`), so it only clears on a real wallpaper change — a **true** same-id rebuild (live toggle / fps tier / media source changes ⇒ key changes) already goes through the `layer-rebuild` branch and its `stopLiveWatch()`; the bare clears in the two early-exit branches (empty id / filtered out) **stay** (nothing syncs any layer after those, so clearing is right). ② the adopt hop, right after `ensureLivePointer(liveFrame)`, re-attaches the active state using the **same readiness predicate as the first-frame gate** (new watch pointing at the same frame + `isConnected` + non-deferred payload + `isEffectivelyPlaying()` + `liveFrameReady()`), instead of waiting for the next heartbeat. ③ the 1 s heartbeat tick restores it unconditionally whenever `responsive` (the scene is really producing frames / the web render page is reachable) is true, logging one diagnostic per watch — putting the **single authority** over that flag back in the heartbeat layer, so any clearing site missed now or added later is corrected on the next beat.
  **Why the self-heal hangs off `responsive` and not the original branch condition**: that condition contains `|| !isEffectivelyPlaying()`, so a paused page walks it too, and a paused scene page has `fps=0` ⇒ `alive` is always false — hanging the self-heal off the branch condition disables it; conversely "set it true unconditionally" would mark a **deliberately paused** render page as active during pauses (pointer injection and the media bridge running hot). Hanging it off `responsive` is right both ways: only a page really producing frames self-heals, and the first beat after playback resumes (≤1 s) heals it.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` gains three structural assertions (the normal-path live clear must be wrapped in `idChanged` with the comparison before the assignment / the adopt re-attach must be **one continuous statement** carrying every readiness predicate / the self-heal must hang off `responsive` and report only once), each with a **negative control**, with the assertion bodies extracted for reuse (the negative controls run the same function, otherwise they prove nothing). For that this file gained a brace-balancing `balancedBody()`: the existing `fnBody()` only cuts to the first `
}`, which for `startLiveWatch` — whose header is followed by an early return — cuts inside the early-exit block and makes the assertion vacuously false.

- **Fix: the update-notice star art showed as a broken image for users who had updated but not yet restarted — the notice now waits until the art is ready before popping** (observed on v1.3.0 release day).
  **Root cause**: the panel bundle is re-read from disk by the host on every page load, while the backend routes only reload when DSH restarts — inside that window the old backend's `/about-qr` whitelist does not have `update-notice.jpg` yet ⇒ the art 404s; if the notice popped as before, users saw a broken image, and one click of "Got it" retires the whole notice forever, so the art would never be seen by anyone.
  **Fix**: before popping, the panel polls `HEAD` on the art path (every 1.5s) and only shows the notice with the art once the new whitelist is live (= the restart happened); after 90 seconds of waiting it degrades to an **art-less** notice with the full text (the star plea also lives permanently on the About tab, so the pop-up is not its only chance). Users who already dismissed send zero probe requests. **The `NOTICE_VERSION` sentinel moves to `1.3.0-r2`**: users who dismissed on release day will see the notice once more (with the art this time).
  **Guards**: `verify-about` gains a §5 art-gate source guard (probe present / gate present / `<img>` only rendered in the ready state / negative controls).

- **New: the sidebar quick panel gains a "Content rating" filter + the search box is capped at 300px**.
  **What**: the view bar gains a content-rating dropdown (All / Everyone / PG13 / Mature / Unrated) sharing the **same key** as the settings page (`contentRatingFilter`, two-way sync — same treatment as the type filter) — the rating gate already lived in the panel list's data source (first of the two filter stages in `playableInventory`); only the control was missing, so a rating change on the settings page shrank the panel list with nothing to explain or change it back. The empty state gains a rating branch ("No playable wallpapers under the \"{name}\" rating"). The search box is capped at 300px on its own row, with the rating/type/view controls right-aligned on a second row — the narrowest official sidebar (276px) structurally cannot fit four controls on one row, so the 2026-10-04 "one row, never wraps even at minimum width" rule is rewritten for the new reality (deterministic two rows; no bare `flex-wrap`, which shreds rows at intermediate widths).
  **Guards**: `verify-scene-live` quick-panel synthetic scope all green; i18n two-way reconciliation (**pure-ASCII copy is not wrapped in `weT`**: "PG13" contains no CJK, the call-site scanner cannot see it, and a wordlist key would be flagged as an orphan — bare literal, no wordlist entry).

- **Audit round: five real defects fixed in "3D depth" — the panel list and "what actually moves" did not share a source, a plugin slot nested inside a native group was missed, the plugin slot default silently disabled easing, the centre crossing jumped 2 device pixels, and the panel readout was not clamped** (source: two **independent** audits run before delivery on the "interface elements follow + plugin front end switch" batch — one reading the behaviour layer line by line, one reading settings / panel / tests / docs; each item below names the audit finding it fixes. **No** shipping switch was changed and **no** stored unit moved).
  **What**: ① **The panel list shares the screen's source** (audit #1; `src/parallax-layer.js` gains `parallaxPluginEffectiveGroups()`): `parallaxPluginGroups()` is only the **raw** candidate layer — "the element children of every `[data-slot]` outlet" — while the candidates that really receive a displacement must pass two more gates: with interface follow on, those contained by a **native group** (the `PARALLAX_GROUP_SELECTOR` entries, e.g. a plugin slot inside a `conversation.view` outlet) are dropped by the outer `contains()`, and those contained by **another plugin's child** are dropped too; `parallaxTargetsRefresh()` and `parallaxDiscoveredGroups()` now share that one function ⇒ the slots listed under Extensions → Plugin front end **are** the ones that get a displacement written (the panel used to list the raw candidates, so a slot nested inside a native group appeared in the list yet never moved — dragging it did nothing, with no way to tell why). ② **A plugin slot's default no longer disables easing** (audit M1): `parallaxMaxPercent()` only walks the **stored keys** while a keyless slot falls back to `PARALLAX_PLUGIN_DEFAULT` ⇒ with the wallpaper distance at 0, interface follow off and only the plugin front end on, it computed `pctMax = 0` and the frame took the "settle in one shot" shortcut, silently killing `parallaxSmooth` (a ~10px jump visible on the easing tail). Now `if (PARALLAX_PLUGIN_DEFAULT > max) max = PARALLAX_PLUGIN_DEFAULT;` — a keyless slot counts as the 1% default. ③ **Only own keys are read** (audit M4): the plugin table is a `map` stored via a shallow `Object.assign({}, v)`, so a key such as `__proto__` becomes the table's **prototype** ⇒ `parallaxTargetRatio()` and `parallaxMaxPercent()` now use `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(st.plugin, rec.slot)` (matching the walk), so a value can no longer be read off the prototype chain. ④ **No jump when crossing zero** (audit L1): `parallaxSnapAxis()` quantises on the absolute value while `prev` is the **signed** integer written last ⇒ on the frame where the cursor crosses the screen centre a −1 flipped straight to +1 (a 2 device pixel step = a visible jitter). The sign is now taken out first and the value returns to zero when `prev` and this one **differ in sign**: `if (prev && (prev > 0) !== (raw > 0)) return 0;`. ⑤ **The panel readout is clamped to the layer's constants** (audit #2): `parallaxPluginPercent()` in `src/ext-parallax.js` ends with `Math.min(PARALLAX_GROUP_DEPTH_MAX, Math.max(PARALLAX_GROUP_DEPTH_MIN, n))` ⇒ an out-of-range stored value (an old profile from a wider domain, a hand-edited one) no longer reads out as 42%. ⑥ **A long slot name no longer breaks the row** (audit #3): `src/styles.js` gives `.we-picker__slider-row .we-picker__label` `flex: 0 1 auto; overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; white-space: nowrap;` (a slot key is someone else's free naming and can be long; it used to push the readout on the right out of the card). ⑦ Copy and comments: the per-slot row tooltip and the card's own explanatory hint (the one shown once slots are found) both gain the caveat that **a group with a `position: fixed` descendant does not move at all** (both languages), and the `parallaxRatios()` doc comment's `1 + value / 100` becomes the implementation's `scale: calc(1 + var(--we-parallax-bg, 0) / 50)` (with the derivation: the largest displacement is multiplier/100 x full screen width, and enlarging by s leaves s/2 x full screen width of margin on each side ⇒ s = multiplier/50).
  **Why**: ① because "what the panel lets you drag" and "what moves on screen" must be the same set — an invariant first broken when plugin groups arrived; ② because it was a leftover of the old assumption "if the wallpaper does not move nothing else may" (plugin groups no longer hang off the wallpaper); ③④ because they are boundaries of the same table / the same quantisation chain (a stored value may be wider than the current domain; a `map` is only shallow-copied, never validated); ⑤⑥ are that boundary's consequences in the display layer. **Deliberately not changed**: the generic `case 'map'` sanitize (shared with `customFrames` / `frameVariants`, where "the table trusts its writer" is intentional and touching it would move two other tables), `parallaxGroupBlocked()`'s "subtree > `PARALLAX_GROUP_SCAN_MAX` (400) ⇒ keep moving" (a deliberate performance gate: freezing a whole block in a long conversation would be a regression), `parallaxPluginDepths` growing without shrinking (pruning would lose the settings of plugins that are not mounted right now), and the panel list being a render-time snapshot (not a live stream).
