  **What was done**: ① **the verdict is a page-level fact**: a new module-level `let noticeArtVerdict = ""` is written once `ready` / `timeout` is known; the component effect reads the cache first and, when a verdict exists, settles immediately and **sends not a single request** (closing and reopening the panel, or switching sessions and coming back, never re-probes). ② **exponential backoff**: the fixed-interval constant `NOTICE_ART_POLL_MS` is retired entirely and the cadence now comes from the pure function `noticeArtSchedule(waits, backoff)` — table `[1500, 3000, 6000, 12000, 30000]`, capped at the last step, with the final tick shortened to the deadline (so the tick that "waits out the full 90 s" really is sent and the fallback follows immediately) ⇒ measured 8 ticks `0 / 1.5 s / 4.5 s / 10.5 s / 22.5 s / 52.5 s / 82.5 s / 90 s` (the old cadence was 61). ③ the gate, "the `<img>` renders only in the ready state" and "a user who dismissed the notice sends zero requests" are **untouched, word for word**.
  **Why**: the flood came from treating a page-level fact as runtime state that has to be re-confirmed on every mount — backoff bounds the density of a single wait, the cache removes the repetition across mounts, and only both together pull the bound from "60 × number of mounts" down to "8 × 1". **Not changed**: the notice still falls back to a picture-less popup after 90 s (an odd install, or a user who simply will not restart, still sees the announcement — just without the art).
  **Guards**: `test/verify-about.mjs` ⇒ **`ABOUT (stars + QR) CHECKS PASSED (87)`** (78 → 87) — a new ⑤b section. It never reads a second copy of the literals: the backoff table, the deadline and the schedule function are all pulled out of the source, and the real function is then **executed** to count how many requests one deadline really produces (8 ticks, 4 ≤ n ≤ 10; the last tick lands within 1 ms of the deadline). "The table exists but the component still schedules at a fixed interval" gets its own wiring assertion (any fixed-cadence shape such as `setTimeout(probe, …)` is rejected — a bare literal `1500` is caught too, since the assertion must not depend on the retired constant's name); the cache assertion requires the **read** and both terminal **writes** to be present (write-only means the cache was built for nothing). Every main assertion ships a **negative control fed into the same function** (the old fixed 1.5 s cadence at 61 ticks must go red). **Independent proof of teeth (mutants, `.test-cache/mutate-artgate.mjs`)**: A reverts the backoff table to `[1500]` ⇒ 2 red (85); B removes the cache **read** ⇒ 1 red (86); C puts the component back on a fixed cadence ⇒ 1 red (86); all three together ⇒ 4 red (83); after `restore` all 87 are green and `git diff --check` exits 0. ⚠️ Every structural assertion reads **comment-stripped code only** (`stripComments`) — the prose above literally contains `NOTICE_ART_POLL_MS` and "fixed interval", so matching against the raw file would let its own comments trip it into a false positive. **Client-only change ⇒ a page refresh is enough** (clearing those 150 red lines on this machine still needs a **DSH restart** to refresh the route whitelist).

- **Fix: some web wallpapers steal DSH's keyboard focus on every click (composer / dropdowns / the bottom-left account menu lose focus) — a "frame-level focus-steal fence" is now injected into the wallpaper document, swallowing `window.focus()` and keeping a count** (source: reproduced by the user — the wallpaper "鲸鱼计划表", workshopid `3800777313`).
  **Symptom**: while the wallpaper plays, a single click anywhere in the host UI makes DSH's own composer, dropdown/select controls and the bottom-left "settings / feedback / sign out" menu lose focus (the window still has focus and `relatedTarget` is null ⇒ focus moved into **another document**). It is **independent of where you click and dependent on the component type** — only controls that must hold keyboard focus show it, everything else looks fine — and **pausing restores it immediately**.
  **Mechanism (closed statically, three steps)**: ① the wallpaper layer is `pointer-events:none`, so the DSH UI consumes the mouse; the client injects every real mousedown into the renderer page from a window-phase capture listener (`src/live-layer.js:1178-1238`) and only while `selection.sceneLiveActive` (`src/live-layer.js:1194`) ⇒ pausing stops the injection, which is exactly "pausing restores it". ② Web wallpapers run in a strict sandbox (`&webSandbox=strict`, `src/live-layer.js:178`), so the renderer page cannot reach the wallpaper document; control can only reach the wallpaper through the web-shim op channel, and the shim **synthesizes** pointer/mouse events via `elementFromPoint` + `dispatchEvent` (`lib/webwallgl/web-shim.js`: `pushPointer` / `elementFromPoint` / `dispatchEvent`) — synthesized events have `isTrusted === false`. ③ To give the keyboard "somewhere to go" in WE desktop mode the author calls `window.focus()` inside a **capture-phase mousedown** handler (that wallpaper's `index.html:4615-4619`, the only `window.focus` call site in the file) ⇒ one click anywhere in the host and the wallpaper frame takes the keyboard.
  **What was done**: new `lib/we-focus-guard.js` — the injected body ships as **source text** (`weFocusGuardSource()` takes the function's own source via `Function.prototype.toString()`, one single source of truth, no hand-copied second string), and `lib/index.js`'s `/scene-files` HTML injection block places it after the shim and before the seed (`<script data-we-focus-guard="host">`, **gated exactly like the shim**: no shim means no injection channel). The fence replaces the **frame-level** `window.focus` with "count + swallow", installing through two legs (plain assignment ⇒ then `Object.defineProperty` creating an own property when the assignment is ignored), failing only as `window.__weFocusGuard.installed === false` and **never throwing** (the body lives inside the wallpaper document, where an exception would destroy the author's scripts); `window.__weFocusGuard = { calls, blocked, allowed, installed, allow }` is the fence's only way to prove itself (one glance in the wallpaper frame's console shows how many steals were blocked), and `allow = true` is an escape hatch for comparison tests. `package.json`'s `files` includes the new module.
  **Why it can all be dropped, and why only frame-level**: this plugin never sends the keyboard into the wallpaper frame — the web-shim has **zero** hits for `keydown`/`keyup`/`keypress`/`KeyboardEvent`, and the renderer page's keydown is only used to unlock audio. So a stolen frame-level focus has exactly one effect: **taking the keyboard away from DSH** (which is why the author built an on-screen keyboard). **Element-level** `HTMLElement.prototype.focus()` is deliberately allowed through: the wallpaper's own cell editors and modal inputs keep working, and that is the focus it genuinely needs. `inert` / `pointer-events:none` are likewise **not** used to wreck the wallpaper's interactivity. The fence also does **not** live under `lib/webwallgl/` (`test/tools/sync-webwallgl.mjs` `rmSync`s that whole directory on every upstream sync) — it must be our own bytes so an upstream refresh cannot overwrite it.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` ⇒ **`ALL SCENE-LIVE CHECKS PASSED (494)`** (483 → 494) — new: ① a **behavioural leg** (`await import` the real module, then run the **real source** through `new Function('window', src)` in a realm that only has `window`) with six assertions: it blocks while the original is never called, re-installing is idempotent, `allow` passes through and counts, **an unwritable prototype property falls back to `defineProperty`**, an own unwritable/non-configurable property reports `installed === false` **without throwing**, plus a negative control (turn "swallow" back into "pass through" ⇒ the core assertion goes false); ② a **wiring leg** (comment-stripped: the tag, `weFocusGuardSource()`, and the order site-root < shim < **guard** < seed) plus its negative control; ③ a shape leg (no `</script`, no `import`/`export`, sourced through `toString()`); ④ two widened e2e assertions (C3 inspects the real response body and requires the fence **before the seed**; C4 requires it too in the response to an `Origin: null` request over a real socket). **Independent proof of teeth (mutants, `.test-cache/mutate-guard.mjs`)**: A removes the injection leg ⇒ **3 red** (C3 e2e + C4 e2e + wiring, `491 passed`); B turns "swallow" back into "pass through" ⇒ **3 red** (behaviour leg + `allow` leg + fallback leg, `491 passed`; its own negative control stays green here because **the mutated tree already is the shape that negative control synthesizes**); C swaps the fence and the seed ⇒ **2 red** (wiring order + C3 order, `492 passed`); D removes the idempotence guard ⇒ **2 red** (idempotence leg + `allow` leg — without the guard a second install replaces the guard object and the old reference becomes a dead object, `492 passed`); B+C+D together ⇒ **5 red** (`489 passed`), and after `restore` all 494 are green with every anchor at ok/0. ⚠️ Mutant anchors must be the **verbatim on-disk line**: the idempotence line carries a trailing comment, so anchoring on `if (w.__weFocusGuard) return;` finds nothing (the script compares whole trimmed lines on purpose).
  **Known residue**: `top.focus()` / `parent.focus()` on a cross-origin WindowProxy cannot be rewritten from the wallpaper document (the same-origin policy allows the call and forbids the rewrite); if such a wallpaper shows up, `__weFocusGuard.blocked` will stay flat while the symptom persists — that is this residue, and intercepting it host-side (at the cost of an edit war with the author page) is out of scope here. **This is a host-side change ⇒ a DSH restart applies it.**

### v1.3.0 (2026-10-06)

> Everything from 1.2.0 through 1.3.0 (published on npm and GitHub Release):

- **New/behavior: the “thinking-block liquid glass” switch becomes three gears — Off / Liquid glass / **Native** (opaque message content, composer keeps its glass)** (user request, settled after two scope passes).
  **What**: new setting key `thinkingNative`, forming three gears with `thinkingGlass`; **Native wins** and the mutual exclusion lives in the gate layer (Native ⇒ the thinking-glass gate attribute is not set; the storage shape is unchanged ⇒ zero migration for existing configs and factory glass presets — the preset key table adopts the new key, missing keys fill as false). The Native gear returns **message content only** — message bubbles and the markdown family (code blocks, inline code, tags, code segments, citations) — to the host’s native opaque surfaces: the 10 taken-over tokens are reverted inside the content subtree (`[data-chat-flow]` / `[data-vcp-rawhtml]`) to values lifted item by item from the host theme package’s design-platform static palette (`var(--dsw-static-*, literal fallback)`, one set per theme); the **conversation canvas and the composer are untouched** — the input card and tool pop-ups keep their glass and the canvas keeps showing the wallpaper. Bubbles drop their frost/sheen in Native; capsule frost/color and thinking-trigger fine-tuning collapse entirely in this gear (the gate CSS does not match ⇒ drawing them would be dead knobs — the standing rule).
  **Why**: beyond Off (translucent, wallpaper through) and Liquid glass (frosted) there was demand for a fully solid gear; the first pass pinned the whole column back to native, which made the composerSeat’s built-in “36px fade to bg-base” decorative underlay pick up the opaque native value inside the subtree and appear on screen as “a black wedge at the input area” (the official Harness shell has no `.dshDesktopConversationSurface` class, so the canvas’ painter was never covered) — after narrowing to the content subtree per the user’s call, the wedge disappears by construction (the underlay is back to invisible).
  **Guards**: `verify-client` three-gear behavioral assertions (a gear pick writes both keys consistently / native mutual-exclusion attributes / trigger independent-config and capsule rows collapse in Native); `verify-presets` key table; `settings-sanitize-golden` re-recorded (zero drift on every other key).

- **Fix: custom font colors “saved, then simply gone after a restart” — two data-loss holes in the persistence chain** (user report).
  **Hole 1 · half pairs dropped wholesale**: font colors are role colors (5 roles × a `{light,dark}` pair). With “set dark separately” on, **filling only one side** (the other left at “follow”) produces a half pair, and all three sanitizers (`readThemeColors`, the token payload builder, `sanitizeFontset`) drop the whole role when one half is missing ⇒ the color **never reaches disk and never applies**, while the panel shows it as picked — after a restart it is “gone” (verified locally: only roles with both sides filled survived on disk). Fix: the `separate` branch of `onThemeColor` fills the empty side with the **current official color** (falling back to the same color when the theme service is unreachable) — the pair is always complete and “only change dark” keeps its intent.
  **Hole 2 · rollback after a failed save**: font values live on the dedicated fontset channel (edit → localStorage cache → `PUT /fontsets/<active id>`), and the “did the last save succeed” dirty flag did not survive restarts — once a PUT failed (most typically: the host was not re-mounted after a plugin update, and the panel’s own advice is “restart DSH and try again”; or quitting too fast), the startup load would **unconditionally overwrite** the newer cached edits with the host’s older values. Fix: the cache now carries a restart-surviving dirty flag (writing the cache marks it dirty; a confirmed PUT writes it clean); on startup, a “dirty cache ≠ host values” state **adopts the cache and pushes it immediately** instead of silently rolling back (trade-off: with two desktops sharing one data directory, the last thing this window showed wins — recorded in the comments).
  **Guards**: `verify-fontset` new ⑦g (half pairs: static assertion + a behavioral record that sanitizers really do drop them) / ⑦h (the four-piece rollback defense + double negative controls).
- **Fix: the plugin market's git install failed (issue #141) — the `prepare` build hook collides with pnpm 11's build-script gate for git dependencies; the build moves to `prepack`**.
  **Symptom and repro**: the market defaults to `dsh plugin --profile web add git+https://github.com/elysia395/dsh-wallpaper-engine.git`, and pnpm 11 answers `ERR_PNPM_GIT_DEP_PREPARE_NOT_ALLOWED` ("needs to execute build scripts but is not in the allowBuilds allowlist") — the install simply fails (reporter: Windows + DSH 0.1.7-rc.2). Reproduced deterministically with one command in an isolated profile here; the npm channel (`dsh plugin … add dsh-plugin-wallpaper-engine`) was verified unaffected.
  **Root cause**: a git install unpacks the sources and runs the package's `prepare` (our client build), while pnpm 11 gates **build scripts of git dependencies** behind its `allowBuilds` safety list (deny by default) — so the install is refused. The old shape (shipping `scripts/prepare.mjs` and documenting that `prepare` really runs at install time) was exactly the shape that walks into that gate.
  **Fix**: the build moves from `prepare` to `prepack` (runs only during `npm pack` / `npm publish`, in the publisher's working tree) — **no script runs at install time at all**; the installed package uses the shipped `lib/client.js` directly (kept honest by CI's build idempotence plus `verify-client-sync`). `scripts/prepare.mjs` no longer ships (zero dev-surface files in the tarball), and the "what ships" / "install-time scripts" wording in the three READMEs, CONTRIBUTING and CODE-STRUCTURE follows. **Immediate workaround for anyone still blocked** (verified working): add the exact `allowBuilds` key pnpm prints to the profile's `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and retry, or use the npm channel.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-package-publish.mjs` ⑦ now pins the new invariant — **none of `prepare` / `preinstall` / `install` / `postinstall` may exist**, and the build must hang off `prepack`; section ②'s ship-whitelist collapses to **empty** (a ratchet that may only tighten), with the negative control flipped (`prepare.mjs` is now judged a dev-surface leak). The three READMEs' ship list matches a real `npm pack --dry-run`.

- **Fix: in Edge, switching video wallpapers did nothing (the old wallpaper stayed on screen forever) — the content gate's mirror-canvas branch never read the draw mark** (user report; repro = open the web instance in Edge, click any video wallpaper: `layer-hold` with no `layer-reveal` ever after).
  **What**: the video branch of `layerContentReady` hard-coded "canvas present ⇒ `return false`" for the Edge mirror-canvas tier (intended as "wait until `weDrawFrame` paints the first stroke", born with the content gate in 7a1e60b), while the painter's mark `canvas.dataset.weDrawn = "1"` (`weDrawFrame` already writes it and calls `noteLayerContent(canvas)`) **was never read** — so the `recheck()` fired by that very first stroke ran straight back into the same predicate, and the release condition could never hold; at `VIDEO_STALL_GIVE_UP_MS` (15s) the guard stops waiting and, per "never show a blank layer", leaves the old wallpaper in place — which reads as "switching does nothing". The branch now reads the mark: `if (mirror) return !!(mirror.dataset && mirror.dataset.weDrawn === "1");`.
  **Why it only broke now**: that branch only applies when the UA contains `Edg/` (only the Edge tier builds a mirror canvas; the desktop shell and other browsers take the native `<video>` tier, whose gate reads `readyState` and always worked). The user runs the desktop app day to day, so it never fired; switching to the Edge-hosted web instance made it unconditional.
  **How it was found**: the on-disk diag showed three switches in a row stopping at `layer-hold` with no `layer-reveal`, and the underlay falling back to the author colour (the frame sampler could not get pixels either) — contradicting "the element has no data". A temporary forensic line (removed in the same batch) printed the gate-time truth: `rs=4 ns=1 vw=1920x1080` (has a frame, has dimensions, merely not playing yet) — leaving the mirror-canvas branch as the only path to the false verdict. Repro/verification ran in the **isolated instance** (profile web-015 + `DSH_WE_DATA_DIR` isolation, no ledger lock): synthetic pointerover (warm) → card click (apply) in headless Edge → never released before the fix, `layer-reveal … held=13ms` after it.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` gains two checks — the gate must read `weDrawn` and the old "canvas present ⇒ false" form must not exist, with a negative control (reverting the read to `return false` turns it red); the painter's `canvas.dataset.weDrawn = "1"` and the gate's read are asserted **in the same batch** — the root cause was exactly two halves that were each written once and never checked against each other.

- **Fix / behaviour: the skin interop now covers the theme dimension — the theme steps aside when a skin takes the stage (our own change is handed back), and an explicit theme switch is never hard-reverted** (user report: "after setting a wallpaper and switching back to the skin, the plugin's leftover environment covers the skin"; their rule: "WE theme — let other themes not cover it; if a theme switch happens, do not hard-cover it back").
  **What**: the yield list grows from "wallpaper layer + the whole glass family" to the theme — (1) all five theme write entries (evaluation / write / image sources / the two frame legs) go quiet while we are yielding (skin on stage owns the theme); (2) the clear path (which both the user's Clear button and the skin yield funnel into, `applySelection("")`) calls a new entry `themeFollowRelease()`: **only the change we made is handed back** — the original value is recorded before our first real write (`themeFollowBefore`) and restored on exit with a diag line; two "do not touch" gates — the yield flag is set (someone changed the theme), or the current value no longer equals the last one we wrote (a change happened outside our view); (3) the application that restores the wallpaper when a skin leaves carries `fromSkinRestore`: theme evaluation **does not reset the yield flag**, so a theme the user switched during the skin window survives that restore — with no such switch the behaviour is byte-identical to a normal wallpaper change; (4) the hand-back itself follows the write discipline (`themeFollowWritten = back` before `setTheme`) — it fires a `theme/change` of its own, and without the bookkeeping that event would read as "someone else took over" and lock the hand-back of that same wallpaper in the yielded state.
  **Why**: the old behaviour left the theme at the wallpaper's verdict during the yield window (the early-return clear path neither evaluates nor hands back), so the whole host UI stayed in the wallpaper's dark/light choice once the skin took over — and the skin's own light/dark variant was dragged along with it. This was the largest visible piece of "the environment was not handed back cleanly". The hand-back undoes **our own** change; it is not a forced revert: the user's or anyone else's switch always wins (the yield flag plus the current-value comparison), and the boundary is pinned in both directions — when nobody touched the theme during the window, the restore still writes the wallpaper verdict (no "never write again" overcorrection).
  **Guards**: `test/verify-theme-follow.mjs` gains section ⑧ (the three hand-back gates · quiet evaluation during the yield while the hand-back still runs · positive/negative controls for `fromSkinRestore` — the same sequence without the flag gets written back to the wallpaper verdict · the hand-back's own event must not read as "someone else took over" · re-selecting the same wallpaper takes over again); `test/verify-client.mjs`'s interop block gains ⑥ (hand-back on the clear path / the five yield gates / the two "do not touch" gates + three negative controls).

- **New: interop with the skin center (dsh-skins) — while a skin is on stage, our wallpaper and the whole glass family step aside** (the opposite direction is already implemented by them: they read `body[data-we-wallpaper]` only and stop drawing the skin while a wallpaper is active; this adds the reverse — when a skin is tried on / applied, we yield, reading their signal only and never writing any of their state).
  **What**: the signal is a single leg — a MutationObserver watching `html[data-dsh-skin]` (their public marker; as of dsh-skins#49 their explicit try-on / apply / **re-apply** all rewrite it, and a same-value `setAttribute` still produces a mutation record). **Enter hysteresis 450 ms** (their startup paint is a transient flip that their own refresh corrects afterwards; a real action stays lit for ≥1.5 s, so only an appearance that survives a beat counts) + **exit hysteresis 2.6 s** (their refresh removes the marker and re-adds it, so a momentary removal is not an exit; later than their 1500 ms yield ceiling). "We are on stage" is decided on **two legs**: wallpaper on stage (marker present ⇒ there is a layer to clear; this leg also guards the startup race — settings in flight or a configured wallpaper whose layer has not been built yet do not count as an action; the field report was "refreshing with a wallpaper set wiped it") and **no wallpaper** (settings loaded and the id is empty ⇒ there is no layer to clear and no race to guard, but the glass family would still sit on top of the skin — glass is orthogonal to the wallpaper (`data-we-glass-page` is mounted unconditionally), so this leg must enter too; otherwise a user without a wallpaper who tries on a skin mid-session never loses the glass, and the install-time adopt only covers the startup moment). Yield = clear the current wallpaper (if any) + the whole glass family stepping out (`applyGlass` strips the seven family attributes at its top; the thinking-glass and sidebar-follow gates in effects follow); rotation is paused first on enter (`rotationEnabled` set to false — otherwise rotation would auto-fill the empty id). Restore happens only after the skin has been **persistently** off: memory puts the wallpaper and the rotation switch back; a manual re-pick during the yield wins (the `fromManual` hook exits the yield immediately); at startup we only "adopt" the current state and write nothing (waiting for `selection.loaded`, since the local cache may be stale). **The yield memory is persisted** (`skinYieldRestoreId` / `skinYieldRestoreRotation`, two UI-less settings keys): enter writes the pre-yield choice to disk, exit consumes and clears it; after a restart the adopt arms from the on-disk memory (no longer an empty one), and if the skin was removed while the app was closed the boot restores straight from memory (`boot-restore` reusing exit's consumption path) — a restart no longer loses the wallpaper and rotation choice. The light polling once added for the "apply" leg (`GET /api/skin-center/v2/active`) was **removed entirely** once their contract landed — pure events, no network. `lib/index.js` also documents the **cross-plugin read contract** (they synchronously read `settings.id` from `config.json` before delivering the document, issue #51 — the env-var name / file name / key are bilateral, and a one-sided rename would throw the first-frame pre-judgment back to "the skin flashes first"). A `skin-yield` diag line (enter / exit / adopt reasons in `~/.dsh-wallpaper-engine/diag/http.jsonl`) keeps the scene replayable.
  **Guards**: the interop block at the end of `test/verify-client.mjs`: (1) single-leg signal (the polling leg must not come back — judged on comment-stripped code) plus the two-leg enter condition (negative controls: dropping the "we are on stage" premise, or regressing to the old single-leg "marker only" condition, both fail); (2) read-only discipline (never write their marker); (3) the yield takes effect at all three glass gate points (applyGlass / the two in effects), the hysteresis constants vs their 1500 ms ceiling, manual re-pick exits immediately; (4) restore follows memory and never overrides a newer user choice (three negative controls).
- **Removed: the Extensions tab's first module, "hardware monitor bars", is retired** (user's call: resource monitoring does **not** belong in this beautification plugin — it should become a **separate monitoring plugin**; the Extensions tab and the other two modules stay as they are).
  **What**: all four halves of the module are deleted — the browser side (canvas layer `src/metrics-layer.js` + island `src/ext-metrics.js`) and the host side (sampler `lib/metrics.js` + the read-only route `lib/routes/metrics.js`) — along with the 29 `metrics*` keys and `parallaxMetrics` in `lib/settings-schema.js` (30 keys), the 21 `onMetrics*` handlers plus the two subscription / teardown wiring points in `src/client.js`, the whole metrics style block in `src/styles.js`, its slice of `src/i18n-copy.js`, and the `lib/metrics.js` entry in `package.json`'s `files`. **The Extensions tab's container semantics did not change by a single line**: `extensionModules()` now returns three items (custom session avatars / click & trail effects / 3D depth) — that is exactly what making it a registry bought. The "the bars take part in the parallax" part of the third module (the bars / labels / rules multipliers and the `parallaxMetrics` key) retires with it, leaving the wallpaper and the mascot (the multipliers on `body` drop from five to two).
  **Why**: (1) it was the only module with **host-side always-on sampling** (a resident `typeperf` child on Windows), which is not the same kind of thing as "beautification"; (2) resource monitoring is a monitoring product surface of its own — as a separate plugin it does not have to compromise from both ends (inside a wallpaper plugin it can only grow on top of the wallpaper, and its controls have to compete with glass / fonts / theme for room).
  **Guards**: the Extensions-tab checks in `test/verify-client.mjs` go from four module cards to three (anchors, count, registry order, and no parameters drawn while all three modules are off); `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` loses three whole blocks (the two browser-side registrations and their "one copy in the bundle" checks, the behaviour checks for the host sampler and the read-only route, and the two-halves checks for the canvas layer and the island), the registry-string expectations become `AVATAR_EXTENSION_MODULE, FX_EXTENSION_MODULE, PARALLAX_EXTENSION_MODULE`, the parallax source checks re-anchor (selector `.we-layer, .we-rope`, `const pctMax = st.bg;`, only two multipliers left on `body`), and the avatar style-slice end marker hangs off the "Extensions module two" heading (it used to be the deleted metrics heading); the settings fixture `test/fixtures/settings-sanitize-golden.json` drops all 30 keys from both the client and host expectations (zero drift on every other key); `docs/ROUTE-INDEX.md` is regenerated from 41 to 40 routes and `docs/GUARD-MAP.md` recomputed (with both modules gone the zero-coverage list is down to the two vendor artifacts); the Chinese / English README, HOW-IT-WORKS and CODE-STRUCTURE follow (module list on the tab, the two legs of the parallax layer, the route diagram and the role table). `npm run verify:all` green (453 scene-live checks).

- **Fixed: the page glass effect no longer requires a wallpaper to be set** (user report: the glass did nothing until a wallpaper was selected).
  **What**: every page-wide glass rule used to hang off the wallpaper gate `data-we-wallpaper` (an attribute the plugin only sets while a wallpaper is selected), so with no wallpaper the Frost / Glass opacity / Glass color / Glass fidelity sliders were dead knobs and the UI stayed at the host's native solid colours. The gate is now split into **two orthogonal anchors**:
  · **`data-we-glass-page` (new — the page-glass anchor)** — mounted **unconditionally** by `applyGlass` in `src/glass.js` (the plugin running means the glass recipe applies; same family as the existing `data-we-glass-chat` / `-window` / `-floaters`). A wallpaper is only one possible **backdrop source** for the glass, never a precondition. The 99 page-wide rules in `src/styles.js` now hang off it: the surface-token mapping (`--dsw-alias-bg-layer-1/2/3`, the elevated button, the code-block family, borders…), the conversation glass (bubbles / composer card / tool popups), the trajectory frost, left-sidebar liquid glass, the native right panel, and both fallback tiers (no `backdrop-filter`, software rasteriser).
  · **`data-we-wallpaper` (unchanged semantics — wallpaper-only concerns left)**: the page **getting out of the way** (`--dsw-alias-bg-base` / `--dsw-specific-sidebar-fill` set to transparent so the wallpaper layer shows through), the light-scheme text contrast boost, the desktop-shell canvas base being cleared (`.dshDesktopFrame`, which would otherwise cover the wallpaper whole), the metrics bar / click-effects layers (they draw the wallpaper's own pixels) and the existing wallpaper-gated wording of #137's "sidebar full transparency".
  With no wallpaper the page does **not** turn transparent: `body` keeps the host's opaque base colour (glass surfaces sit on top of that base and the content), and the recipe still computes a readable base (readability floor + frost + sheen + hairline). With a wallpaper both anchors are present at once, so the look is byte-identical to before. Unload is paired too: `clearEffects` in `src/effects.js` removes the new anchor alongside -chat / -window / -floaters.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-readability.mjs`'s whole surfaceSpecs table (39 text-bearing surfaces) and every `ruleFor` lookup re-anchor to `body[data-we-glass-page]` (F2a still demands the readability floor on each of them, with its negative control; F2e's fence predicate moves too); `test/verify-glass-compositing.mjs`'s G8 re-anchors and gains a **coverage floor** (light/dark 23/23 — which also surfaced a latent defect in that scanner: `lastIndexOf('{', open)` matched `open` itself, so the block header was always empty and the check had been passing vacuously; the new floor caught it), and S2c2 re-anchors; `test/verify-client.mjs` gains three: the mapping must sit on `body[data-we-glass-page]`, the wallpaper block must contain **no** glass mapping (negative control: anchoring it back to the wallpaper gate fails), and a runtime check that the anchor stays `on` after the wallpaper is cleared through the real picker (while `data-we-wallpaper` disappears); `test/compat-harness-pages.mjs`'s surface-token probe now reads the anchor-present side first and temporarily removes the anchor for the control side (it used to add the wallpaper anchor itself; the plugin mounts the new one all the time); `test/verify-glass-surfaces.mjs`'s gate-licence note and the two coverage notes in `test/fixtures/harness-ui-surfaces.json` follow. `verify:all` is green.

- **New: the mascot can use a custom imported picture** (System tab · chat mascot; user request: custom import, and importing again overwrites the previous one).
  **What**: the "Mascot form" row gains a **third card** (user request: after an import it should sit after the whale-lady card with the same card size and style): its stage box matches the tallest built-in card (64×96), clicking it imports or replaces (a re-import overwrites), and it becomes the `--active` card while in use. The artwork of the mascot on the main page (`RopeDock`) now resolves through one helper, `ropeArtOf(sel)` — a non-empty `mascotImage` with a valid display box uses **the user's picture**, otherwise it falls back to the built-in form selected under "Mascot form" (maid / whale). While a custom picture is active the two built-in form cards are **disabled** (picking one would change nothing, and "clicking does nothing" is worse than a disabled control, so the disabled state also fades out); clearing the custom picture re-enables them, and the "Clear" button only appears at the end of that row while a custom picture exists. The new route family `lib/routes/mascot.js` (POST import / GET·HEAD view / DELETE clear) mirrors the avatars but holds **exactly one** picture: the import clears the previous siblings first, so a re-import overwrites. Files live under the plugin data directory's `mascot/` (`mascot-<stamp>.<ext>`) and settings only carry the file name (the `mascotFile` kind). The display box (`<w>x<h>`) is computed at import time by fitting the picture into 96×192 (small pictures are not upscaled, except for a 28px floor on the short side so tiny icons stay clickable; the box is computed from **the stored picture's** post-scale pixels — `downscaleImageFile`'s width/height contract is "post-scale pixel counts") and stored as `mascotImageBox` — dragging, edge snapping and viewport clamping all read it; when the box is invalid `ropeArtOf` falls back to the built-in form wholesale (better a built-in than measuring a mascot with a guessed box). The 96×192 ceiling has a **single source in the schema** (`MASCOT_BOX_MAX_W/H`): the `mascotBox` kind bounds values by it (a hand-edited config cannot smuggle in a `999x999`), and `ropeArtOf` enforces the same ceiling as a backstop. The import chain (file pick / decode / 512px downscale / POST / failure copy) is **shared** with the avatars (`pickImageFile` / `downscaleImageFile` / `postImageAsset` / `assetRouteFailure`).
  **Guards**: the real-socket route block in `test/verify-scene.mjs` now covers both user-image-asset families — six checks for the mascot (404 + no-store before an import / 415 for a non-whitelisted MIME / 200 + name shape on import / byte-identical + long cache on view / **a re-import overwrites** (new name, exactly one file left) / 404 again after a clear); `test/verify-contracts.mjs` adds mascot-MIME parity plus four route-shape checks (shared reader + atomic write / exactly one picture / long cache + 404 no-store / directory and cap); `test/verify-client.mjs` judges the mascot section both before an import (only "Import image…" plus a placeholder, no "Clear", the form cards still clickable) and after one (the hint, replace + clear, the form cards disabled) at the source level. The settings fixture gains `mascotImage` and `mascotImageBox` in the same pass.

- **New: custom session avatars — the FIRST module of the Extensions tab — making task messages read like a chat between friends** (user request: off by default; once on, import custom images for both sides, circular by default, with adjustable size and corner roundness).
  **What**: `src/avatar-layer.js` (new) is a **chat-DOM patch** — it gives the three message-row kinds (`data-chat-flow-kind` = `user` / `steering` / `assistant-step`) a single avatar node (a round face whose width is the avatar size), turns the row into a horizontal flex and reverses the user row, so your messages sit on the right with your avatar and the assistant's on the left. It only reads `selection`; its MutationObserver exits early twice (first "is it a message row", then "is it already inside a decorated row"), so the per-frame insertions of streaming cost one `closest` each. Turning the switch off strips every injected node and row marker, disconnects the observer and removes the body attribute plus both CSS variables (byte-for-byte back to native). Size and roundness travel through `--we-avatar-size` / `--we-avatar-round`, so dragging a slider is a variable swap that rebuilds nothing. The default avatars are drawn inline SVGs (a person for you, a four-point star for the assistant — one parts table feeds both the DOM and the panel), and an imported image simply replaces one. There is **no custom name line**: the intermediate version (one per side, the assistant's defaulting to the current model name and yours to "Me", drawn above the avatar) was removed wholesale on user feedback ("it looks ugly") — settings keys, node, styles and copy are all gone and must not come back.
  **Where the images live**: the host stores them under the **plugin data dir** in `avatars/` (`<side>-<stamp>.<ext>` — not `overrides`, which holds per-wallpaper frames, and not `uploads`, which would pollute the wallpaper inventory); settings only carry that file name (the `avatarFile` kind rejects anything else) — it doubles as the "is one set" truth and the `<img>` cache key (a replacement changes the name, so stale caches never win). New route family `lib/routes/avatar.js`: POST import (MIME whitelist + 8 MB cap, using the **shared body reader and an atomic write** rather than a new streaming exemption), GET/HEAD to view (long cache backed by the file name; 404 + no-store when unset) and DELETE to clear; the client downscales to 512px on a canvas before uploading. Five new settings keys: `avatarEnabled` (off by default), `avatarSize` 40 (24–72), `avatarRadius` 100 (0–100, 100 = a full circle), `avatarUserImage`, `avatarAiImage`; two more for the custom mascot picture, `mascotImage` and `mascotImageBox`, joined in the same pass.
  **Import-failure copy**: when the host has not been re-mounted (host modules load once at start, while the browser bundle is re-fetched on every reload) the POST falls through to the SPA fallback and comes back as a **bare 405** — "the host returned 405" on screen says nothing useful. `src/client.js`'s `avatarFailureReason` now separates the two failures by the presence of the `{ error }` envelope: a bare 404/405 means "the host has no avatar route: restart DSH and try again (host-side changes need a remount; refreshing the page is not enough)", matching the font-set and system-fonts wording.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-client.mjs` now checks the four module cards **in registry order** (avatars first) plus the avatar module drawing no parameters while off, drawing both sides and the two sliders when on, their ranges and readouts (40px / 100%), the two "Import image…" buttons (no "Clear" before an image is set), and the absence of any custom name row; `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` gains two registration checks (exactly one copy of each half in the bundle), a source-level block for the decorator (row selectors and side map, gate attribute and the two variables, the absence of any custom name line, the six cleanup steps, the two-level observer early exits, read-only settings), the island's visible behaviour (only the master switch while off; both sides and both sliders once on), a real run of the decorator against a minimal DOM stub (on ⇒ only the two message kinds get a node, a settings change updates in place, off ⇒ byte-for-byte back to native) and the pure-function surface (no throw without a DOM, URL building); `test/verify-contracts.mjs` adds avatar-MIME parity across the halves (client `accept` vs host `AVATAR_EXT`) plus five route-shape checks (two sides only / shared reader + atomic write / sibling cleanup on replace / long cache + 404 no-store / directory and cap); the settings fixture `test/fixtures/settings-sanitize-golden.json` gains the five keys in the same pass (safety valve: zero drift on every other key; the two custom-name keys tried midway were dropped from the fixture along with the feature), and `test/verify-scene.mjs` gains a **real-socket** route-level case (its data directory is isolated through `DSH_WE_DATA_DIR` and deleted afterwards — the route clears the sibling files of the same side, so pointing it at the real directory would delete the user's avatars): 404 + no-store before an import / 400 for a bad side / 415 for a non-whitelisted MIME / 200 + name shape on import / 200 + byte-identical + long cache on view / HEAD without a body / a replacement leaving exactly one file / 404 again after a clear.
- **"Presets" becomes an explicit advanced-only feature** (ADR-0008 D4). The preset block already happened to be absent from the sidebar, but only as an **implicit fact**: the sidebar ctx never passed `glassPresets`, and the renderer's entry guard `if (!presets) return null` skipped the whole block — so no assertion ever covered it, and adding that field to the sidebar ctx would have made the block appear **silently**. Now made explicit in three places: (1) a gate in `src/glass-panel.js` (`!sidebarSurface && renderGlassPresetsBlock(...)`); (2) `glassPresets` joins `src/quick-panel.js`'s **setting-only placeholders** (drop the gate ⇒ the renderer throws on its very first destructure); (3) `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` pins **both** variants (the sidebar's label sequence *and* whole-tree text must contain no preset block; the settings variant must — that case gained a `ctxOver` injection, without which the assertion would be vacuously false).
  **Why advanced**: a preset is neither a master switch nor a per-surface override but a **cross-surface bulk overwrite** — one preset is a complete glass snapshot, and applying it assigns the whole set with **no confirmation and no undo** — while deleting a factory preset is **permanent** (tombstone, no recovery path). By D4's orientation that belongs on the advanced side.
  Also corrected the upstream sentence claiming "the settings page and the sidebar quick panel share the same preset block" (they never did).

- **The sidebar's Appearance page is back to drawing only the *simple* configuration** (user call; decided by `ctx.surface`; **ADR-0008 D4** restored and refined). **What**: the quick panel's Appearance page no longer draws each surface's "independent configuration" layer **or its parameters**, nor the capsule-blur / capsule-colour rows under the thinking-glass switch — those are once again **settings-page only**; the **master switches** stay in both variants (thinking-glass, left-sidebar glass, sidebar glass, sidebar full-clear, sidebar follow-global) together with the global four (glass colour / transparency / blur / fidelity).
  **Why**: the sidebar is a narrow, "tweak it in passing" surface, while overriding the global values per surface is an advanced action. ⚠️ The 2026-10-03 pass reversed this gate ("both variants show the same content") **without amending ADR-0008's D4** ⇒ the docs (that ADR + `UPGRADING.md`) and the implementation disagreed from that day on; this version takes the ADR as authoritative and writes the classification into D4.
  **Two layers of safety**: (1) the gate lives in `src/glass-panel.js` (`!sidebarSurface`) and the advanced rows are not even **built** in the sidebar variant; (2) their handlers sit in `src/quick-panel.js`'s **setting-only placeholders** (using one throws) ⇒ moving an advanced row back into the sidebar **blows up loudly** instead of silently becoming a knob that does nothing.
  **Guards**: ordered control-label sequences for both variants in `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` (10 rows sidebar / 18 settings) plus new two-way D4 assertions — the sidebar variant must have **zero** "independent configuration" rows and no preset block yet must keep both master switches, and the settings variant must have them. ⚠️ This also fixed a **dead guard**: the boundary check lived in the old `CASES` loop while the appearance cases had moved to `MORE_CASES`, so it could never fire (it stayed green when the classification changed); it now lives in the loop that actually runs those cases.

- **Fixed: loose-directory scene wallpapers could not render live** (user report: loose-form scene wallpapers would not render).
  **What**: `lib/index.js`'s `sceneFieldsFor` drops the old "live render is pkg-only" gate — the stale
  assumption was that WebWallGL's httpSource can only fetch a single-file container, so scene dirs whose
  entry is a `.json` were marked `sceneLive:false` and got no token. Since WebWallGL 2.1.0 the renderer
  natively supports the loose form: the site root is identical for both forms (`<mediaBase>/<token>/`),
  the renderer fetches `project.json` first and judges the form by the `file` suffix (`.json` → loose:
  fetch the entry json and sibling assets; otherwise fall back to the `scene.pkg` container), and in the
  auto form it tries sceneDir first with scenePkg as the fallback. So the token is now issued for every
  scene and the form judgment belongs to the renderer; `isPkg` keeps a single purpose (`scenePkgBytes` —
  a loose directory has no "whole package", so the first-frame budget stays at baseline). Two stale client
  comments updated (`src/media-prep.js` / `src/client.js`; the logic itself only ever read the flag).
  **Guards**: `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` gains a true-loose fixture (project.json declaring scene.json +
  the entry and assets loose on disk, zero scene.pkg in the directory) with seven checks — inventory marks
  `sceneLive:true` + token, the token decodes to the entry json's absolute path, `/scene-files` serves each
  piece 200 + byte-identical (project.json / entry scene.json / sibling main.tex), and a negative control
  ("no scene.pkg ⇒ 404"); ROUTE-INDEX regenerated.

- **Both wallpaper lists (sidebar + settings-page library) switch to "placeholder spacer + visible window" virtual scrolling, and the library view no longer pages** (user: "load everything, hide nothing" / "no pagination").
  **What**: the sidebar's 100-row cap plus the "plus N more not shown" hint, and the library view's pager (normal/hidden page state x2, page-flip callbacks x4, the pager row), are retired wholesale; both places share one mechanism — `qpVirtWindow` in `src/quick-panel.js`: scrollHeight is propped up by top/bottom spacer rows to the **full-collection** height, so scrolling / search / filter semantics are identical to the full DOM. Windows are counted in **grid rows** (list = 1 per row; cards = cols per row with the column count measured back from the first rendered card's track width) and render 6 extra rows beyond the viewport so fast scrolling never shows a gap; a view/tag change (normal / draft / hidden / drill-down) renders one "unmeasured" frame (first window of 30 items + zero spacers) and the measurement effect immediately follows. Hooks live in the WallpaperPicker component and reach the pure renderer `renderPickerModal` via ctx (gridRef / vwin); the "✕ close" card is grid item 0 and must be part of the window slice; the hidden view's header/question rows move out of the grid (semantic rows are not grid cells — only then does the row math hold); the classic CD-rack layout is **not** virtualized (aspect-ratio overlapping cards change row height with column width, so the fixed-pitch math doesn't hold, and that layout never paged) and keeps full rendering. Official shell (list scrolls itself) and drawer shell (ancestor scrolls) share one measurement formula; scroll events merge via rAF, unchanged windows don't re-render; the official shell resets scrollTop to 0 on view/count changes.
  **Why**: libraries can run into the thousands — full DOM stutters and paging contradicts the "load everything, hide nothing" requirement; a window computed per item never fills the viewport (in the card grid one scroll row shifts the window by one item — hit in testing), so it must be folded through grid rows.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-picker-model.mjs` cross-checks the "virtual first window" instead of the page slice (the rig's React is a stand-in, the measurement effect never runs ⇒ first window = close card + min(30, playable), a deterministic assertion); `test/verify-client.mjs` swaps the pagination assertions for virtual-window ones (30 cards / no pager in the library view / out-of-window items not rendered but reachable via search + negative controls), walks the class sequence Fragment-transparently (the virtual window's Fragment produces no DOM), and re-records the goldens for the virtual-window shape (normal view 181 tokens); `scripts/build-client.mjs` inline markers updated (QP_LIST_MAX retired; qpFindScroller / qpVirtWindow on duty).

- **Fixed: the "Mascot size" slider also resized the form cards inside the settings page** (user: the mascot in
  the settings page must not change size — the slider should only affect the mascot on the main page).
  **What**: `src/panel-tabs.js` renders the mascot form cards at their fixed `ROPE_FORMS` base size
  (chibi maid 52×57 / whale 64×96; the `* sel.ropeScale` multiplication is gone) and the hint now reads
  "Fixed-size cards; the size slider only affects the mascot on the main page". The main-page `RopeDock`
  (the pull-cord mascot itself) keeps its scaling.
  **Why**: the slider's job is sizing the desktop mascot; the cards in the settings page are just form
  pickers — scaling them is unnecessary, and at high factors (up to 2.5×) they grow very large.
  **Guards**: `test/verify-client.mjs` asserts, with the slider set to 1.5, that both card artworks keep
  their fixed base sizes (turning red if the multiplication ever comes back).

- **Fixed: the sidebar's "Wallpaper properties" panel was cut off and unscrollable on wallpapers with many
  properties** (user report: opening the properties panel on a wallpaper with a lot of properties shows only
  part of them).
  **What**: `src/styles.js` gives the drill-down container its own scroll inside the official sidebar shell
  (`.we-qp--official .we-qp__propsview--drill { overflow-y: auto; overscroll-behavior: contain; scrollbar-gutter: stable; }`).
  **Why**: the host gives the tab content area a fixed height with `overflow:hidden` (and the wallpaper tab
  additionally carries `--library`, which deliberately delegates scrolling to the list) — but the properties
  drill-down has **no list**, so without its own scroll the panel is clipped, and between it and `body` there is
  **no ancestor the user can actually scroll** (an `overflow:hidden` box can still be `scrollTop`-ed
  programmatically, but the user cannot scroll it). In the drawer shell the scrolling is done by
  `.we-repo-panel__body`, so this rule only applies to the official shell and does not stack a second scroll.
  **Guards**: the sidebar scroll-chain section of `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` gains "the panel scrolls itself /
  it has a bounded height / the premise: the content area clips" plus a negative control (dropping `overflow-y`
  turns it red); the real-browser rig `test/tools/sidebar-props-scroll-rig.mjs` (real bundle + real React + the
  host geometry: a wallpaper with 60 properties, scrolling each candidate container to verify the last row is
  reachable — FAIL before the fix, PASS after, with the wallpaper list's own scrolling unaffected).

- **The first module's defaults are now the maintainer's own tuned set** (user's words: "make the settings
  currently in the hardware monitor bars extension the default configuration" ⇒ clarified as "bake the
  values I have dialled in now into the defaults").
  **What**: ten keys in `DEFAULTS` of `lib/settings-schema.js` changed value — height / vertical offset /
  bar width / row gap / threshold / opacity / the black-band opacity / glow / outline width (the numbers
  live in that file and are not copied here). The key set and their order are unchanged, every other
  `KINDS` range is untouched, and the other nineteen keys already matched
  the tuned set (horizontal offset 0 / bar gap 2 / thin white rules on / blend mode Auto / smoothness 60 /
  window 60 / solid bars on / series names on / three series on, network and disk off), so they stay put.
  ⚠️ **The master switch stays default-off** (`metricsEnabled: false`, kind still `boolFalse`): in the same
  batch it briefly defaulted to **on** (`DEFAULTS` flipped to true and the kind to `boolTrue` — a kind
  encodes the "value when absent", so changing `DEFAULTS` alone would be sanitised back), and it was then
  **reverted to off** on the user's testing verdict (see the next entry: all three extension modules ship
  off and are turned on on demand).
  **Why**: these ten are not factory guesses but the look that was dialled in by hand — baking them in means
  a user who *turns the module on* lands on that look instead of re-dragging every slider; whether to turn
  it on stays the user's call.
  **Guards**: the "Extensions" block of `test/verify-client.mjs` pins **all three modules default-off**
  (no parameter controls drawn by default + `checked === false`), then walks the master switch's `onChange`
  and requires the whole parameter list (placement / bar shape / threshold / the four look controls / bar
  colours / series names / five series) to appear, with "turning the master switch off collapses that list"
  alongside; in `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` the "off" half of the extension-island comparison passes an
  **explicit** `metricsEnabled: false` (it no longer hands `DEFAULTS` in as the "off" sample, which would
  turn that comparison into a tautology the moment a default flips); the golden fixture was
  re-stamped to the new defaults (`.test-cache/regen-golden-metrics.mjs` gained a ten-key `RESTAMPED` list:
  the key set is unchanged and only values moved, so it re-stamps instead of adding slots — it reported
  `added 0 key slots across 18 cases`, with every other key acting as the zero-drift safety valve).
  **No new setting key** (count, order and ranges unchanged) ⇒ `docs/ROUTE-INDEX.md` and `lib/types/` stay
  untouched.

- **All three extension modules now ship switched off and are turned on on demand** (user call, 2026-10-04):
  `metricsEnabled` went back from default-on to **default-off** (click & trail / 3D depth were already off) —
  none of the three decides for the user any more; you turn on what you want.
  **Guards**: the "Extensions" block of `test/verify-client.mjs` moved from "default on ⇒ parameters drawn"
  back to "default off ⇒ not one parameter is drawn", pinning `checked === false` for all three modules;
  `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` uses an **explicit** `metricsEnabled: false` as the "off" half of the
  extension-island comparison (rather than handing `DEFAULTS` in); the golden fixture was re-stamped to the
  new defaults (`metricsEnabled` and its kind back to `boolFalse`) with every other key as the zero-drift
  safety valve; both language versions updated together.

- **Optimised: the cost of the "3D depth" motion** (user feedback: the effect is good, but the motion
  costs too much).
  **What**: all of it lives in `src/parallax-layer.js` and the parallax section of `src/styles.js`, and
  **no new setting key** was added (so the golden fixture and `docs/ROUTE-INDEX.md` stay untouched).
  ① **Narrower variable scope**: the per-frame displacement step `--we-parallax-x / -y` moved from the
  body to **the layers that actually move** (`.we-layer` / `.we-rope` / `.we-metrics`, rescanned with
  `document.querySelectorAll` every 250ms, each record holding the step it last wrote so nothing is read
  back from the DOM), leaving only the five per-layer multipliers on the body (written once per settings
  change) — custom properties inherit, so writing the step on the body re-resolved styles for the whole
  document every frame. ② **Frame rate capped at 60Hz** (`PARALLAX_MIN_FRAME_MS` = 0.75 of a frame): the
  extra frames on a high-refresh display now only reschedule instead of writing variables, and the easing
  was always normalised to 60fps. ③ **"Arrived" is now judged by visible displacement**:
  `settle = max(PARALLAX_SETTLE_PX, remaining visible pixels / largest multiplier)` — 0.25px normally and
  1px once the cursor has been still for 180ms, which retires the last dozen frames of the tail in one go
  (under 1px of movement at the moment it snaps, so nothing shows). ④ **The viewport is read only on
  start and on resize** (no more asking `window` every frame). ⑤ **No frame at all when every multiplier
  is 0** (one placement and done). ⑥ **Compositor layers only while moving**: the loop adds
  `.we-parallax--moving` to those layers (the stylesheet gives it `will-change: translate` — translate
  only, so scale's raster scale is never frozen) and it is removed the moment the loop settles or the
  module stops — this repo deliberately keeps no always-on compositor layer (same reasoning as the
  two-frame nudge of `.we-layer--repaint`).
  **Why**: this layer lives entirely on the input path, so its cost is exactly three things — how wide
  each frame's style invalidation reaches, how many frames one gesture runs, and whether every frame has
  to repaint the full-screen wallpaper. Those six items cut one of the three apiece, and not a single
  pixel of the effect itself changed.
  **Checks**: the source-shaped check in `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` gains a ⑧ performance group (scoped
  writes / 60Hz cap / visible-displacement thresholds / zero-multiplier fast path / the compositor hint
  being added and dropped), plus a new **behavioural** check that builds a minimal fake DOM
  (`querySelectorAll` returning three fake elements, a fake rAF driven by hand) and really runs frames,
  asserting that the step lands on those elements only, that the body carries just the five multipliers,
  that the compositor hint class is present while moving and gone once settled, and that a stop cleans up
  both sides; the `src/styles.js` check gains
  `body[data-we-parallax="on"] .we-parallax--moving { will-change: translate; }`.
- **Added: a third Extensions module, "3D depth" — parallax that follows the cursor**.
  **What**: five new setting keys (`lib/settings-schema.js`, DEFAULTS + KINDS) — `parallaxEnabled`
  (`boolFalse`, off by default), `parallaxBg` (`num 0..10`, default `1`, wallpaper and mascot),
  `parallaxMetrics` (`num 0..20`, default `1`, the bars layer), `parallaxMascot` (`boolTrue`) and
  `parallaxSmooth` (`num 0..98`, default `85`). Two new files: behaviour layer `src/parallax-layer.js`
  (a base module: zero `ctx`, reads `selection` only) and descriptor `src/ext-parallax.js` (a five-row
  island); `src/panel-tabs.js` gains a third registry entry; `src/client.js` gains five named handlers,
  the `extensionCtx()` entries and `subscribe(syncParallaxLayer)` / `disposeParallaxLayer()`;
  `src/styles.js` gains a "third Extensions module: 3D depth (parallax)" section; `src/i18n-copy.js`
  registers 12 keys; the build table gains both modules.
  **It builds no DOM node at all**: it only listens passively to `pointermove` on `document` and `resize`
  on `window`, turning the cursor's offset from the screen center into seven CSS variables plus a
  `data-we-parallax` switch attribute on the body, and the stylesheet multiplies everything with `calc()`
  — `.we-layer` translates by `--we-parallax-bg` and scales by `calc(1 + var / 100)`, `.we-rope` by
  `--we-parallax-mascot`, `.we-metrics` by `--we-parallax-metrics`, and `.we-metrics--labels` /
  `.we-metrics--guides` take the bars value plus 1% / 2%. One unit: for a cursor offset `u` from the
  screen center the target displacement is `PARALLAX_DIRECTION × u × percentage / 100` (crossing the whole
  longest diagonal moves that layer by its own percentage of that diagonal — exactly the user's
  definition); `PARALLAX_DIRECTION = -1` means **mirrored about the screen center** (opposite the cursor);
  the easing is an exponential approach per frame (`parallaxSmooth`, `0` = instant) and the loop stops once
  the target is reached (zero frames while idle; only `parallaxKick()` schedules one).
  **Why**: the user's request — "add an extension: 3D effect: as the cursor moves, the components and the
  wallpaper background ease along the direction symmetric about the center… the background travel defaults
  to 1% of the longest diagonal, and [hardware monitor bars] also supports a custom travel (chart component
  only, its other components add 1% each on top of that value)… click & trail effects must not shift… make
  the mascot widget take part too, if possible". Three trade-offs: ① the displacement uses the **CSS
  independent properties `translate` / `scale`** rather than `transform` — the wallpaper transition's
  `resetLayerSwitchStyles()` writes and clears an inline `transform`, and only the independent properties
  compose with it instead of overwriting each other; ② the wallpaper must be **scaled up** by
  `1 + pct / 100` (max horizontal displacement = pct × viewport width / 2, so without the up-scale the base
  color shows at the edges); ③ the **click & trail layer deliberately does not take part** (the user's third
  point), nor does the drawer / quick panel / settings panel (they sit under the pointer, so moving them
  would ruin aiming).
  **Checks**: `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` gained two registration checks (each new module appears once in
  `INLINE_MODULES` and once in the bundle), a single-file import of `src/parallax-layer.js` with three
  `syncParallaxLayer()` states (on / out-of-range values / off) plus `disposeParallaxLayer()` — **no rAF in
  the environment, zero throws, zero DOM side effects** — a source-contract check (the direction constant
  and both formula lines, the `typeof document` guard in `parallaxBody()`, the `requestAnimationFrame` early
  return, `LABEL_STEP` / `GUIDE_STEP`, both passive listeners, plus **negative** assertions that it builds no
  DOM and never touches `we-fx`) and a styles-section check (the five `body[data-we-parallax="on"]` rules, the
  `translate` / `scale` `calc()`, plus **negative** assertions that this section contains no `transform:` and
  no `.we-fx`), and the third module's island check (order-sensitive: off ⇒ only the master switch row, on ⇒
  the four parameter rows); `test/verify-client.mjs` gained the island behaviour assertions (three module
  cards, master switch off by default, the four row labels and slider ranges 0..10, 0..20, 0..98, the default
  readouts `1%`, `1%`, `85%`, the mascot switch on by default, and a negative assertion that no other module's
  controls leak in).
  **Fixtures**: five new setting keys ⇒ `test/fixtures/settings-sanitize-golden.json` re-recorded by
  "behaviour at introduction" (`added 165 key slots across 18 cases`); `docs/ROUTE-INDEX.md` untouched
  (pure client side, no new route).
- **Fix: Auto blend on an almost black background now uses Lighten with the bars' opacity cut down (the factor is customizable)**.
  **What**: the Auto stop in `src/metrics-layer.js` goes from two tiers to three — new
  `METRICS_BLEND_NIGHT = 'lighten'` / `METRICS_LUMA_DEEP = 0.15`; the
  **first** line of `metricsBlendForLuma` tests for almost black (actually-shown luma < `METRICS_LUMA_DEEP`)
  ⇒ that band takes Lighten, and the **bars layer** (`band.host.style.opacity`) has its opacity multiplied by
  a customizable factor (the user's "opacity -50%"). Each entry of the per-band plan is therefore `{ mode, dim }`
  instead of a bare string, and `dim` comes **only** from that almost-black rule — a hand-picked Lighten carries
  no `dim` (the user's own choice is not cut, so the flag cannot be derived from `mode === 'lighten'`); the
  fallback used when the wallpaper cannot be sampled is the new `metricsBlendFallback()` (never cut); the
  **label layer and the rules layer keep the plain opacity** (they always blend `normal`, so cutting them would
  only turn white text and lines grey). The island's Blend mode hint and both word lists follow.
  The factor itself is a **setting key**: the user then asked "the opacity -50% just mentioned should also be
  customizable", so the hard-coded `METRICS_DEEP_ALPHA = 0.5` became the new key `metricsDeepOpacity`
  (`50` by default, range `10..100`, `kind: 'num'`), read in the frame as
  `const deepAlpha = metricsClamp(selection.metricsDeepOpacity, 10, 100, 50) / 100;`. The key states the
  **result** (how much opacity the bars layer keeps on an almost black background) rather than "how much to
  subtract" — `100` naturally means "turn this almost-black rule off", so no extra switch is needed; the island
  row is called **"Bar opacity on black"** and appears **only while Blend mode is Auto** (no other stop can ever
  pick the almost-black rule, so showing it there would lie — the same reason the five colour pickers only show
  up in "Distinct hues").
  **Why**: the user's report — "fix [hardware monitor bars]: for an almost black background, Auto should use
  Lighten + bars opacity -50%". On pure black `multiply` is ×0 (it wipes the bars out) and `overlay` barely
  lifts them; only `lighten` (per-channel max) lets a bar show its **own colour** — but a full-strength Lighten
  glows into a mush, one smeared band, hence the opacity cut (50% by default, adjustable). The threshold `0.15` is a look-and-feel value and
  must stay **far below** `METRICS_LUMA_SPLIT` (`0.5`), otherwise it swallows the dark tier.
  **Checks**: the `metrics-layer.js` source-contract check in `test/verify-scene-live.mjs` grew 8 assertions (the
  two constants, the first-line test, `metricsBlendFallback`, the hand-picked `{ mode: manual, dim: false }`, the
  `deepAlpha` line that reads the setting key and the `entry.dim ? deepOpacity : opacity` write); the Extensions
  block of `test/verify-client.mjs` checks the new slider's range (10..100), its default read-out (`50%`) and that
  it shows up **only on Auto** (switching to another stop hides it, switching back brings it back). **One new
  setting key** ⇒ the golden fixture was recorded as it behaves on introduction (`added 33 key slots across 18
  cases`); `docs/ROUTE-INDEX.md` stays untouched (client-only, no new route).

- **New: the second Extensions module — click & trail effects**.
  **What**: ① two new files: the island `src/ext-fx.js` (one master switch plus a sub-switch each for clicks
  and the trail, with their own style, size, glow, duration, width, opacity, blend mode and colours — either
  sub-switch alone is enough, and turning one off folds its own group of controls away) and the canvas layer
  `src/fx-layer.js` (a body-level `.we-fx` canvas at `z-index: -1` that only listens **passively** to
  `pointermove` / `pointerdown` on `document` — the host is `pointer-events: none`, so it captures no input;
  clicks whose target is one of the plugin's own controls are dropped, so clicking a button never also bursts
  a ripple). ② Clicks draw two spreading rings (the second starting 18% of the lifetime later), a cluster of
  **unequal** flying dots plus one white flash, or both; the trail is either a round-capped band that tapers
  segment by segment or dots left behind at a fixed step — both fade by age and are dropped once spent, so a
  resting pointer still fades out. ③ The frame loop is **content-driven**: `fxStart()` returns immediately when
  there is no `requestAnimationFrame` (hence zero DOM side effects in the test sandbox), it only keeps
  scheduling while something is alive and stops itself once the canvas is empty (zero frames while idle); right
  after the loop's only early return it calls the idempotent `fxEnsureHost()`, so the deadlock the bars layer
  once shipped (treating "host not created yet" as a reason to return) cannot come back. ④ Opacity and the
  **blend mode** are written on the **host element** (on the canvas they would only blend against the host's
  own stacking context) and the layer composites with `lighter` inside; the colour follows the theme accent /
  rainbow (a hue per instance that also drifts over time) / custom. ⑤ Stacking comes from document order: the
  layer sits above the wallpaper and below the scrim and the bars, re-checked every frame with
  `compareDocumentPosition`. ⑥ 14 new `fx*` setting keys (`lib/settings-schema.js`: DEFAULTS + KINDS + four
  value tables; the master switch defaults to **off**), and the swatch picker only appears in the Custom mode.
  **Why**: the user asked to start the next extension — "click effects and trail effects", explicitly as an
  example, free to design. The layer deliberately **never samples wallpaper pixels** (hence no Auto mode) and
  leaves blending to the default Screen or a hand-picked mode; every other choice aims at looks first (two
  rings instead of one, unequal dots instead of equal, age-based removal instead of smearing).
  **Tests**: the Extensions block in verify-client pins two module cards, no parameter labels while the master
  switch is off, then 34 labels / ten slider bounds / the `140px` · `420ms` · `85%` readouts / five blend-mode
  options / a swatch picker that only appears in Custom mode and defaults to `#4f8cff` / each sub-switch
  folding away its own group; verify-scene-live pins that each new file appears exactly once in the bundle,
  that `fx-layer.js` imports standalone with **no throws and no side effects without rAF** (its export set is
  exactly `disposeFxLayer,syncFxLayer`), the source form (`FX_HOST_ID`, the content-driven loop, the
  `fxEnsureHost()` right after `if (!fxOn || !st.on) return;`, both `insertBefore` stacking paths,
  `mix-blend-mode` and `opacity` on the host, `globalCompositeOperation = 'lighter'`,
  `target.closest(FX_UI_SELECTOR)`, `{ passive: true }`) and the island's 12 parameters in order with the
  swatch picker only in Custom mode. Docs (README / HOW-IT-WORKS / this file) are mirrored between the two
  languages; `docs/ROUTE-INDEX.md` is untouched (client-only, no new route); the i18n dictionary and the golden
  fixture pick up the new keys.

- **Fix: Auto blend mode now reads the brightness actually on screen, the three host families stay above the
  scrim, and the rules layer sizes its own canvas**.
  **What**: ① Auto no longer judges by the wallpaper's **raw image** brightness — `drawImage` returns source
  pixels, which neither see the CSS filters this plugin issues nor the darkening layer stacked on top of the
  wallpaper, so a bright wallpaper dimmed to mid grey still counted as bright and took multiply (bars = bar
  colour × dark backdrop, i.e. lost in the background). The plugin's own effects are now undone first
  (`metricsDisplayLuma`: the `brightness()` factor, `contrast()` stretched around 0.5, the fade colour and the
  leaf opacity of a translucent wallpaper, then the `scrim` darkening — every step clamped to the schema
  ranges), those four keys plus the fade colour feed a signature (`metricsDisplaySig`) so dragging a setting
  recomputes the displayed brightness immediately instead of waiting for the sampling cache, and the sampled
  raw values are kept as `metricsLumaRaw` while everything downstream sees the displayed one. ② The three host
  families (bars / labels / rules) and the darkening layer `.we-scrim` are all `z-index: -1` body-level
  overlays, so their stacking comes from document order — and the scrim is only appended **when a wallpaper
  becomes active**, so a layer that starts earlier (extensions on at boot, wallpaper applied later) ended up
  covered by it; the layer now checks document order every frame and moves the three families back above the
  scrim in their original order (`metricsRaiseAboveScrim`). ③ The rules layer **never sized its canvas**: the
  band canvases and the label layer both called `metricsSizeCanvas`, the rules one did not, so its bitmap
  stayed at the HTML default 300×150, stretched by the CSS `width/height: 100%` — with a block narrower than
  300 only the left part of each rule was drawn, taller than 150 the lower rules were cut off and everything
  was stretched vertically (the user's report: the white rules did not show fully). Both layers now size their
  canvas whenever the geometry changes and clear their own repaint signature.
  **Why**: the user's report — ① the bars are hard to make out because on bright backgrounds multiply is
  applied and then the background plugin's own darkening dims them further; the suggestion was to read the
  values this plugin has configured, compute the area's actual displayed brightness from them, and pick the
  mode from that; ② the white rules did not show fully.
  **Tests**: the canvas-layer source assertions in verify-scene-live now cover `metricsDisplayLuma` /
  `metricsFadeBaseLuma` / `metricsDisplaySig` / the four `selection.*` keys that go into the reversal /
  `metricsRaiseAboveScrim` and its per-frame call / the "clear my signature when sizing succeeds" shape of
  both layers (asserting that a line was painted cannot catch a missing size call). **No new setting keys**, so
  the i18n table and the golden fixture are untouched; the `METRICS_BLEND_VALUES` comment in
  `lib/settings-schema.js` now says the decision uses the displayed brightness. Chinese and English docs
  (README / HOW-IT-WORKS / this file) kept in sync.

- **Fix + new: the row labels get their own layer with their ink clamped to 20%–80% lightness, and Auto
  blend mode now switches per band of wallpaper brightness**.
  **What**: ① the series names move **out of** the blending bars layer into a third host layer
  (`#we-metrics-labels`) that always blends `normal` — the previous version kept the labels in the bars'
  layer, where `multiply` crushed a near-white theme colour into near-invisibility (the user's report: the
  labels had become hard to make out, likely because they are white and multiply wiped them); the colour is
  still the Appearance one but passes through `metricsClampInk()`, which clamps the HSL **lightness** into
  20%–80% (`METRICS_INK_MIN` / `METRICS_INK_MAX`) — pure white lands on 80%, pure black on 20%, and the text
  reads on both bright and dark wallpapers; ② Auto no longer takes one average brightness for the whole
  block: it is cut horizontally into at most `METRICS_BAND_MAX` bands (**one host per band, each carrying its
  own stop**), each band samples the wallpaper brightness behind its own bars and picks multiply for bright
  areas and overlay for dark ones — CSS `mix-blend-mode` is element-level, so per-pixel switching is
  impossible and bands are the approximation; the cuts always fall in the middle of a bar gap
  (`metricsBands`), so no bar is ever sliced in half; a band that cannot be sampled (iframe, cross-origin,
  no video frame yet) still falls back to the last hand-picked mode; the probe changed from a 24 × 24 square
  to a single row of `bands × 1` pixels, its cache key carries the band count and the block's horizontal
  position/width share, and the band count is **decided by geometry alone** (manual mode is always one band,
  so the DOM is never rebuilt per frame).
  **Why**: the user asked ① to clamp the label colour's lightness to 20%–80% while still letting the plugin
  control it, and ② whether it could be smarter and detect regions whose background brightness is above or
  below 50%, using multiply on bright areas and overlay on dark ones.
  **Guards**: verify-scene-live's bundle check went from "two independent paint paths" to **three** (bars /
  labels / rules exactly once each — collapsing them back would mean the three layers stuck together again
  and the decoupling is gone), and its canvas-source assertions gained `METRICS_LABEL_HOST_ID` /
  `METRICS_BAND_MAX` / `METRICS_INK_MIN` / `METRICS_INK_MAX` / `metricsClampInk` / `metricsBlendPlan` /
  `metricsBands` / `metricsSyncHosts` / `metricsPaintLabels` / the per-band blend write
  (`metricsNodeStyle(band.host, 'mix-blend-mode', mode)`) / the gradient using the clamped ink.
  **No new setting keys** ⇒ neither the copy table nor the golden fixture changes. Chinese and English docs
  (README / HOW-IT-WORKS / this file) are in sync.

- **New: the resource bars can set their layer blend mode (including an automatic mode that reads the
  wallpaper's brightness), a thin-white-rule scale, and a custom colour per series**.
  **What**: ① a new setting key `metricsBlend` (an enum, default `auto`) decides how the bars fuse with the
  wallpaper — the manual stops are CSS `mix-blend-mode` (normal / multiply / overlay / screen / soft-light /
  darken / lighten), while **Auto** samples the wallpaper image and picks multiply when the sampled average
  is bright and overlay when it is dark; when the image cannot be sampled (Web and Scene wallpapers are
  iframes, the video has no frame yet, a cross-origin canvas is tainted) it **falls back to the last mode
  picked by hand** instead of something arbitrary, and the sample is cached with a TTL so pixels are not
  read every frame; ② a new setting key `metricsGuides` (thin white rules, default on) draws a 1px white
  line across the whole block at **each row's 50% height** and **between every two rows** (a constant 35%
  opacity), turning the bars into a readable scale; ③ the canvas layer now mounts **two host layers** —
  bars and row labels in one (the blend mode and opacity are written on that one) and the white rules in
  **their own** (blending stays `normal`: in the bars' layer multiply would wipe the lines out); the rules'
  repaint signature **excludes the data timestamp**, so the per-second bar frames never redraw them, and
  the two layers' visibility and blending stay independent — exactly the seam that the third extension
  module (**3D depth**, `src/parallax-layer.js`) later shipped on; ④ the "Distinct hues" mode no longer uses built-in fixed hues: five new setting
  keys `metricsColorCpu` / `metricsColorMem` / `metricsColorGpu` / `metricsColorNet` / `metricsColorDisk`
  (type `hex`, defaulting to the old factory hues, so anyone who never touched a colour sees the same
  picture) take over, and the panel only grows the five colour pickers in that mode (preset dots plus a
  custom colour wheel whose drag writes live and whose release persists).
  **Why**: the user asked ① whether the layer blend mode of this overlay could be changed ("multiply on a
  bright background, overlay on a dark one"), ② to "add thin white lines, one at each bar chart's 50%
  height and one between every two bars", ③ to "keep the labels/bars and the white lines decoupled,
  because the next extension I plan is a cursor-driven 3D depth effect", ④ to "allow custom bar colours".
